####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\README.md
####################################################################################################





> A **LangGraph**-powered, six-node agentic NL-to-SQL engine for banking
> term-deposit compliance analytics — served via a **Streamlit** UI.
> Ask natural-language questions; receive precise SQLite results.

---

## Table of Contents

1. [Quick Start](#quick-start)
2. [Architecture](#architecture)
3. [Configuration](#configuration)
4. [Running the Application](#running-the-application)
5. [Adding a New Database](#adding-a-new-database)
6. [Documentation](#documentation)

---

## Quick Start

```bash
git clone <repo-url>
cd COMPLIANCE-CHATBOT



pip install -r dlh_ai/requirements.txt

streamlit run dlh_ai/application/app.py
```

The browser opens automatically at **http://localhost:8501**.

---

## Architecture

### Component Layers

```
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║  PRESENTATION LAYER                                              ║
║                                                                  ║
║   application/app.py  (Streamlit)                                ║
║   • LLM Configuration page — test & persist DLH credentials      ║
║   • Chat page — conversational NL2SQL with stage-pill trace      ║
║   • Session state stores processor & chat history                ║
╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║  ORCHESTRATION LAYER                                             ║
║                                                                  ║
║   ComplianceProcessor  (genai/core/processor.py)                 ║
║   • Builds AgentState from the user question                     ║
║   • Calls compiled LangGraph graph                               ║
║   • Maps final AgentState → response                             ║
╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║  PIPELINE LAYER  (LangGraph StateGraph)                          ║
║                                                                  ║
║   Node 1   IntentChecker    rule-based, no LLM                   ║
║   Node 2   SchemaChecker    rule-based, no LLM                   ║
║   Node 3   SQLWriterAgent   ★ only LLM call in the pipeline      ║
║   Node 4   SQLReviewer      rule-based, no LLM                   ║
║   Node 4.5 SQLValidator     sqlglot AST, no LLM                  ║
║   Node 5   ExecutorAgent    rule-based, no LLM                   ║
╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║  INFRASTRUCTURE LAYER                                            ║
║                                                                  ║
║   utils/config.py    Pydantic Settings — reads .env              ║
║   utils/logger.py    get_logger() — reads logging.toml           ║
║   utils/registry.py  YAML loader + SQLite helpers + DB registry  ║
║   utils/helpers.py   stateless stdlib helpers                    ║
║                                                                  ║
║   genai/config/*.toml   TOML config (no secrets)                 ║
║   genai/prompt/*.yaml   YAML prompt files (LLM wording)          ║
╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║  STORAGE LAYER                                                   ║
║                                                                  ║
║   data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db   SQLite — read-only at runtime   ║
║   data/logs/compliance_bot.log   Rotating log file               ║
║   llm_config.json                Persisted LLM credentials       ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
```


```
[START]
   │
   ▼
intent_checker  ──(intent_ok=False)──► clarify_node ──► [END]
   │ (intent_ok=True)
   ▼
schema_checker
   │
   ▼
sql_writer_agent  ◄──────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                                         │ retry
   ▼                                                         │
sql_reviewer ──(passed)──► sql_validator ────────────────────┤
   │                            │                            │
   │ (max retries / error)      │ (valid)    (invalid → retry)
   ▼                            ▼
executor_agent ◄──────── executor_agent ──► [END]
```

| Node | Module | LLM? | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| `intent_checker` | `genai/nl2sql_agent/intent_checker.py` | ✗ | Rule-based intent gate + normalisation |
| `schema_checker` | `genai/nl2sql_agent/schema_checker.py` | ✗ | Live PRAGMA schema + TOML descriptions |
| `sql_writer_agent` | `genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_writer_agent.py` | ✓ | Single LLM call — SQL generation |
| `sql_reviewer` | `genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_reviewer.py` | ✗ | Safety + structural + table existence |
| `sql_validator` | `genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_validator.py` | ✗ | sqlglot AST + dialect check |
| `executor_agent` | `genai/nl2sql_agent/executor_agent.py` | ✗ | Read-only SQLite execution |

---

## Configuration


Copy or create `.env` at the project root and set your DLH credentials:

```dotenv
MODEL__PROVIDER=dlh
MODEL__MODEL_NAME=google/gemma-4-31b-it
MODEL__DLH_BASE_URL=http://http://10.190.236.15:9000//api/v1
MODEL__DLH_API_KEY=EMPTY
MODEL__DLH_VERIFY_SSL=false

DATABASE__DB_FOLDER=data/comp_db
DATABASE__DB_NAME=COMP_REPORTS.db

LOG_LEVEL=INFO
LOG_DIR=data/logs
```

> **Tip:** LLM credentials can also be entered and saved interactively from the
> **LLM Configuration** page in the Streamlit UI — they are stored to
> `llm_config.json` in the project root and loaded automatically on next launch.

### Config Files

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `genai/config/databases.toml` | ★ DB + table metadata registry (columns, few-shots) |
| `genai/config/query.toml` | Row-limit policy (`enforce_default_limit`, `default_limit`) |
| `genai/config/llm.toml` | LLM provider defaults (overridden by `.env`) |
| `genai/config/application.toml` | App display name |
| `genai/config/logging.toml` | Rotating file-handler configuration |

---


```bash
streamlit run dlh_ai/application/app.py
# or Use launcher.py
```

The Streamlit server starts on **http://localhost:8501** (or CDSW_APP_PORT).

### Workflow

1. Open the **LLM Configuration** page in the sidebar.
2. Enter your **Model Name**, **DLH Base URL**, and **API Key**, then click **Test & Save**.
3. On success the sidebar shows **● Backend Active** and you are redirected to **Chat**.
4. Type a compliance question (e.g. *"How many accounts from Mumbai circle have KYC flag N?"*) and press **Enter**.
5. The six LangGraph nodes run; stage pills light up as each node completes.
6. Results are displayed with the SQL query, agent trace, and a **Download as CSV** button.


---


**No Python code changes are needed.** Edit `genai/config/databases.toml` only:

```toml
[databases.MY_NEW_DB]
db_file     = "data/comp_db/MY_NEW_DB.db"
description = "Description of the new database."

[databases.MY_NEW_DB.tables.MY_TABLE]
description  = "Plain-English summary for the LLM prompt."
filter_query = "SELECT * FROM MY_TABLE WHERE BRANCH = '{branch}'"

[databases.MY_NEW_DB.tables.MY_TABLE.columns]
COL1 = "Human-readable column 1 description"
COL2 = "Human-readable column 2 description"

[databases.MY_NEW_DB.tables.MY_TABLE.few_shots]
examples = """
Question: Show all rows where COL1 is active.
Response:
<begin_sql_response>
SELECT * FROM MY_TABLE WHERE COL1 = 'active';
<end_sql_response>
"""
```




## Design Principles

* **One LLM call per query** — only `SQLWriterAgent` calls the LLM; all other nodes are deterministic and fast.
* **YAML-first prompts** — all LLM wording lives in `genai/prompt/*.yaml`; change messages without touching Python.
* **Zero-code database adds** — add a new database by editing `databases.toml` only.
* **Read-only safety** — every SQL execution is wrapped in a `BEGIN DEFERRED` transaction that is unconditionally rolled back.
* **Defence in depth** — SQL passes through three independent validation layers (regex review → sqlglot AST → SQLite error handling) before results are returned.
* **Persistent LLM config** — credentials entered in the UI are saved to `llm_config.json` and survive Streamlit restarts.

readme = "README.md"
requires-python = ">=3.11"
authors = [
    { name = "IBM GenAI Team" }
]
keywords = ["compliance", "nlp", "sql", "llm", "banking", "streamlit", "langgraph"]

[tool.ruff]
target-version = "py311"
line-length    = 100

[tool.ruff.lint]
select = [
    "E",      # pycodestyle errors
    "W",      # pycodestyle warnings
    "F",      # pyflakes
    "I",      # isort
    "B",      # flake8-bugbear
    "UP",     # pyupgrade
    "SIM",    # flake8-simplify
    "RUF",    # ruff-specific
    "TRY",    # flake8-tryceratops
    "PLC",    # pylint convention
    "PLR",    # pylint refactor
    "PLW",    # pylint warning
]
ignore = [
    "E501",     # line too long — handled by formatter
    "E402",     # module-level import not at top — Streamlit pattern requires this
    "TRY003",   # long raise messages — intentional for user-facing errors
    "TRY201",   # bare re-raise — correct pattern (raise without name in except)
    "TRY300",   # consider else block — style preference
    "TRY400",   # use logging.exception — we intentionally use .error to suppress traceback
    "BLE001",   # blind Exception catch — intentional in UI/backends
    "B008",     # function call in default arg — Pydantic patterns
    "B904",     # raise-from in except — legacy code, tracked in FIXME comments
    "PLC0415",  # import not at top level — lazy imports intentional (optional deps)
    "PLR0911",  # too many return statements — complex validation node
    "PLR0912",  # too many branches — legacy complexity
    "PLR0913",  # too many arguments — existing public APIs
    "PLR0915",  # too many statements — legacy complexity
    "PLR0917",  # too many positional args — existing public APIs
    "PLR2004",  # magic value in comparison — numeric constants in data generators
    "PLW0603",  # global statement — module-level singleton pattern (logger, registry)
    "PLW1510",  # subprocess.run without check — HDFS CLI calls intentionally unchecked
    "RUF001",   # ambiguous unicode — existing content strings
]

[tool.ruff.lint.per-file-ignores]
# Notebooks: generated code, magic values and unsorted imports are expected
"data/datapush_read.ipynb" = ["PLR2004", "I001"]

[tool.ruff.lint.isort]
known-first-party = ["dlh_ai", "genai", "utils", "data", "application"]

[tool.mypy]
python_version = "3.11"
strict = false
ignore_missing_imports = true

FILE: dlh_ai\application\app.py

import base64
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import pandas as pd
import streamlit as st

_PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

CONFIG_FILE = _PROJECT_ROOT / "llm_config.json"

if str(_PROJECT_ROOT) not in sys.path:
    sys.path.insert(0, str(_PROJECT_ROOT))

from genai.core.model import ModelLoaderService  # LLM client wrapper
from genai.core.processor import ComplianceProcessor  # pipeline orchestrator
from genai.nl2sql_agent.graph import build_nl2sql_graph  # LangGraph factory
from utils.config import get_settings  # pydantic settings
from utils.logger import get_logger  # centralised logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.streamlit")



def save_config_to_disk(model_name: str, base_url: str, api_key: str) -> None:
    """Write LLM credentials to llm_config.json next to the workspace root."""
    try:
        # Build the config dict with the three user-supplied values
        config = {
            "model_name": model_name,
            "base_url":   base_url,
            "api_key":    api_key,
        }
        # Overwrite (or create) the file - no sensitive data in git
        with open(CONFIG_FILE, "w") as f:
            json.dump(config, f)
        LOGGER.info("Configuration saved to disk successfully.")
    except Exception as e:
        # Log the error but never crash the UI - the user can retry
        LOGGER.error(f"Failed to save config to disk: {e}")


def load_config_from_disk() -> dict | None:
    """Read and return the saved LLM credentials, or None if the file is missing."""
    try:
        if CONFIG_FILE.exists():
            # Open the file and parse JSON into a plain dict
            with open(CONFIG_FILE) as f:
                return json.load(f)
    except Exception as e:
        # If the file is corrupt or unreadable, return None gracefully
        LOGGER.error(f"Failed to load config from disk: {e}")
    return None



def get_font_base64(font_path: Path) -> str | None:
    """Read a .ttf font file and return it as a base64 string for CSS injection."""
    try:
        with open(font_path, "rb") as f:
            data = f.read()
        # base64-encode the raw bytes and decode to a plain string
        return base64.b64encode(data).decode()
    except Exception as e:
        # Font is cosmetic - log and fall back to the system sans-serif
        LOGGER.error(f"Could not load font at {font_path}: {e}")
        return None


font_dir      = _PROJECT_ROOT / "application" / "fonts"
font_reg      = get_font_base64(font_dir / "Urbanist-Regular.ttf")
font_medium   = get_font_base64(font_dir / "Urbanist-Medium.ttf")
font_bold     = get_font_base64(font_dir / "Urbanist-Bold.ttf")
font_semibold = get_font_base64(font_dir / "Urbanist-SemiBold.ttf")


st.set_page_config(
    page_title="SBI Compliance Chatbot",
    page_icon="",
    layout="wide",
    initial_sidebar_state="expanded",
)



def _font_face(b64: str | None, weight: int) -> str:
    """Return a @font-face rule for Urbanist at the given weight."""
    if not b64:
        return ""
    return (
        f"@font-face {{ font-family: 'Urbanist'; "
        f"src: url(data:font/truetype;base64,{b64}) format('truetype'); "
        f"font-weight: {weight}; font-style: normal; font-display: swap; }}"
    )

font_face_css = (
    _font_face(font_reg,      400)
    + _font_face(font_medium,  500)
    + _font_face(font_semibold, 600)
    + _font_face(font_bold,    700)
)

st.markdown(f"""
<style>
    /* ── FONT DECLARATIONS ────────────────────────────────────────────── */
    {font_face_css}

    # Start on the config page so the user sets up the LLM before chatting
    st.session_state.page = "llm-config"

if "messages" not in st.session_state:
    # Chat history - list of dicts with role/content/meta/stages keys
    st.session_state.messages = []

if "processor" not in st.session_state:
    # ComplianceProcessor instance - None until LLM is connected
    st.session_state.processor = None

if "model_service" not in st.session_state:
    # ModelLoaderService instance - None until connection test passes
    st.session_state.model_service = None

if "last_csv" not in st.session_state:
    # Bytes of the last query result as CSV - used by the download button
    st.session_state.last_csv = None



def initialize_pipeline(model_name: str, base_url: str, api_key: str):
    """
    Build and connect the full NL2SQL pipeline.

    Steps:
      1. Copy default ModelSettings and override with user-supplied values.
      2. Create a ModelLoaderService and call load() to init the HTTP client.
      3. Call test_connection() - sends a tiny "Reply with OK." request.
      4. Build the compiled LangGraph from build_nl2sql_graph().
      5. Wrap in a ComplianceProcessor and return (service, processor).
    """
    try:
        # Start from defaults defined in utils/config.py ModelSettings
        base_settings = get_settings().model.model_copy(deep=True)

        # Override only the values the user supplied in the form
        model_settings = base_settings.model_copy(update={
            "provider":       "dlh",
            "model_name":     model_name,
            "dlh_base_url":   base_url,
            "dlh_api_key":    api_key,
            "dlh_verify_ssl": True,
        })

        # Create the HTTP client wrapper for the DLH inference server
        model_service = ModelLoaderService(settings=model_settings)
        model_service.load()             # initialises the OpenAI-compatible client
        model_service.test_connection()  # sends "Reply with OK." to verify auth

        # Resolve the SQLite database path from settings
        db_path = get_settings().database.db_path

        # Build and compile the 6-node LangGraph StateGraph
        compiled_graph = build_nl2sql_graph(model_service=model_service, db_path=db_path)

        # Wrap the graph in the thin orchestrator that maps API models ↔ AgentState
        processor = ComplianceProcessor(compiled_graph=compiled_graph)

        return model_service, processor

    except Exception:
        # Log full traceback for debugging, then re-raise so the UI shows an error
        LOGGER.exception("Pipeline initialization failed")
        raise

    # Brand block at the top of the sidebar
    st.markdown("""
        <div class="sidebar-brand">
            <div class="sidebar-brand-title">SBI Compliance Chatbot</div>
            <div class="sidebar-brand-sub">LangGraph &middot; NL2SQL &middot; DLH On-prem LLM</div>
        </div>
    """, unsafe_allow_html=True)

    # Page navigation buttons
    if st.button("\u2699  LLM Configuration", use_container_width=True):
        st.session_state.page = "llm-config"

    if st.button("\u203A\u203A  Chat", use_container_width=True):
        st.session_state.page = "chat"

    st.divider()

    # Backend status badge - green when pipeline is live, red when not configured
    if st.session_state.processor:
        st.markdown(
            '<div class="status-badge online">'
            '<span class="status-dot online"></span>Backend Active</div>',
            unsafe_allow_html=True,
        )
    else:
        st.markdown(
            '<div class="status-badge offline">'
            '<span class="status-dot offline"></span>Backend Offline</div>',
            unsafe_allow_html=True,
        )

        st.markdown("""
            <div class="card-header">
                <div class="card-icon">&#x2699;</div>
                <div>
                    <div class="card-title">Model Connection</div>
                    <div class="card-subtitle">Enter DLH inference server credentials and click Test & Save.</div>
                </div>
            </div>
        """, unsafe_allow_html=True)

        with st.form("config_form", clear_on_submit=False):
            # Three credential fields - rendered inside the card
            model_name = st.text_input("Model Name",   value=def_model,
                                       placeholder="google/gemma-4-31b-it")
            base_url   = st.text_input("DLH Base URL", value=def_url,
                                       placeholder="http://host:9000/api/v1")
            api_key    = st.text_input("DLH API Key",  value=def_key,
                                       type="password", placeholder="********")

            st.markdown("<div style='height:4px'></div>", unsafe_allow_html=True)

            btn_col1, btn_col2 = st.columns([1, 1])
            with btn_col1:
                # Primary button: test the connection and save credentials on success
                submit = st.form_submit_button(
                    "\u25BA  Test & Save", type="primary", use_container_width=True
                )
            with btn_col2:
                # Secondary button: reload saved values from disk
                if st.form_submit_button("\u21BA  Reload Saved", use_container_width=True):
                    st.rerun()

            if submit:
                with st.spinner("Testing LLM connection..."):
                    try:
                        # Attempt to build the full pipeline (raises on auth/network failure)
                        svc, proc = initialize_pipeline(model_name, base_url, api_key)

                        # Store the live pipeline objects in session state
                        st.session_state.model_service = svc
                        st.session_state.processor     = proc

                        # Persist credentials so they survive hot-reloads
                        save_config_to_disk(model_name, base_url, api_key)

                        st.success("[OK]  LLM connection successful \u2014 credentials saved.")
                    except Exception as e:
                        st.error(f"[ERR]  Connection failed: {e!s}")

    # ── RIGHT COLUMN: saved config preview ───────────────────────────────────
    with col2:
        st.markdown("""
            <div class="card-header">
                <div class="card-icon">&#x2261;</div>
                <div>
                    <div class="card-title">Saved Configuration</div>
                    <div class="card-subtitle">Credentials stored locally - never committed to git.</div>
                </div>
            </div>
        """, unsafe_allow_html=True)

        if saved_cfg:
            # Render each credential as a structured info row
            masked_key = "********" if saved_cfg.get("api_key") else "(empty)"
            st.markdown(f"""
                <div class="card-accent">
                    <span class="section-label">Active Credentials</span>
                    <div class="info-row">
                        <span class="info-key">Provider</span>
                        <span class="info-val">dlh</span>
                    </div>
                    <div class="info-row">
                        <span class="info-key">Model</span>
                        <span class="info-val">{saved_cfg.get("model_name","-")}</span>
                    </div>
                    <div class="info-row">
                        <span class="info-key">Base URL</span>
                        <span class="info-val">{saved_cfg.get("base_url","-")}</span>
                    </div>
                    <div class="info-row">
                        <span class="info-key">API Key</span>
                        <span class="info-val">{masked_key}</span>
                    </div>
                </div>
            """, unsafe_allow_html=True)

            # Also show the raw JSON in the dark code block for quick copy-paste
            json_data = {
                "provider":     "dlh",
                "model_name":   saved_cfg["model_name"],
                "dlh_base_url": saved_cfg["base_url"],
                "dlh_api_key":  "********",  # never display the real key in the UI
            }
            st.markdown(
                f'<span class="code-label">JSON Preview</span>'
                f'<div class="code-block">{json.dumps(json_data, indent=4)}</div>',
                unsafe_allow_html=True,
            )
        else:
            # Nothing saved yet - prompt the user to fill in the form on the left
            st.markdown("""
                <div class="card" style="text-align:center; padding: 36px 24px;">
                    <div style="font-size:36px; margin-bottom:12px; opacity:0.4;">&#x2205;</div>
                    <div style="font-size:15px; font-weight:600; color:#374151; margin-bottom:6px;">
                        No Configuration Saved
                    </div>
                    <div style="font-size:13px; color:#6b7280; line-height:1.6;">
                        Fill in the form on the left and click<br>
                        <strong>Test &amp; Save</strong> to store your credentials.
                    </div>
                </div>
            """, unsafe_allow_html=True)


# PAGE: CHAT
elif st.session_state.page == "chat":

    # Page header banner
    st.markdown("""
        <div class="app-header">
            <div>
                <div class="app-header-title">Compliance Intelligence Chat</div>
                <div class="app-header-sub">Six LangGraph nodes: Intent &#x2192; Schema &#x2192; SQL Writer &#x2192; SQL Review &#x2192; Validator &#x2192; Executor</div>
            </div>
            <span class="app-header-badge">&#x25CE; CHAT</span>
        </div>
    """, unsafe_allow_html=True)

    # ── Stage pipeline track ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
    # One pill per node; highlights active nodes from the most recent query.
    stages_list = [
        "Intent Checker",
        "Schema Checker",
        "SQL Writer",
        "SQL Review",
        "SQL Validator",
        "Executor",
    ]

    # Extract stage tags from the last bot message (or empty if no messages yet)
    last_stages = st.session_state.messages[-1].get("stages", []) if st.session_state.messages else []

    # Build the HTML pill track - label only, no icon characters
    pill_html = '<div class="pipeline-track">'
    for label in stages_list:
        # Map "SQL Writer" -> "sql_writer" to match stage tag format
        is_active = any(label.lower().replace(" ", "_") in trace.lower() for trace in last_stages)
        cls = "stage-pill active" if is_active else "stage-pill"
        pill_html += f'<span class="{cls}">{label}</span>'
    pill_html += '</div>'
    st.markdown(pill_html, unsafe_allow_html=True)

    # ── Chat history ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    # Re-render every stored message on each script re-run.
    if not st.session_state.messages:
        # Empty state placeholder when no conversation has started yet
        st.markdown("""
            <div class="chat-empty">
                <div class="chat-empty-icon">&#x25CE;</div>
                <div class="chat-empty-title">Start a Compliance Query</div>
                <div class="chat-empty-sub">
                    Ask a natural-language question about the compliance data.<br>
                    Example: <em>"How many senior citizen accounts have KYC flag N?"</em>
                </div>
            </div>
        """, unsafe_allow_html=True)
    else:
        for msg in st.session_state.messages:
            if msg["role"] == "user":
                # User bubble - right-aligned navy gradient
                st.markdown(
                    f'<div class="chat-row"><div class="msg-user">{msg["content"]}</div></div>',
                    unsafe_allow_html=True,
                )
            elif msg["role"] == "bot":
                # Bot bubble - left-aligned white card
                st.markdown(
                    f'<div class="chat-row"><div class="msg-bot">{msg["content"]}</div></div>',
                    unsafe_allow_html=True,
                )
            elif msg["role"] == "notice":
                # Error or clarification notice - colour depends on type
                type_cls = "msg-notice-blocked" if msg["type"] == "error" else "msg-notice-clarify"
                st.markdown(
                    f'<div class="{type_cls}">{msg["content"]}</div>',
                    unsafe_allow_html=True,
                )

            # If this message has a metadata block (trace, SQL, data), render it
            if "meta" in msg:
                st.markdown(
                    f'<span class="code-label">Agent Trace &middot; SQL &middot; Results</span>'
                    f'<div class="code-block">{msg["meta"]}</div>',
                    unsafe_allow_html=True,
                )

    # ── CSV download button ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
    # Shown only when the last query produced result rows
    if st.session_state.last_csv:
        st.download_button(
            label="\u2193  Download Results as CSV",
            data=st.session_state.last_csv,
            file_name="compliance_results.csv",
            mime="text/csv",
            key="global_csv_btn",
        )

    # ── Chat input ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    # st.chat_input appears fixed at the bottom of the page.
    # It returns None until the user submits a message.
    if prompt := st.chat_input("Ask a compliance question - e.g. How many accounts in Mumbai circle?"):
        if not st.session_state.processor:
            # Pipeline not ready - direct the user to the config page
            st.error("\u2699  Please configure the LLM connection first (sidebar \u2192 LLM Configuration).")
        else:
            # Add the user's message to the history immediately
            st.session_state.messages.append({"role": "user", "content": prompt})

            with st.spinner("Agents collaborating..."):
                try:
                    # Build the Pydantic request model - fixed question_id per session
                    from genai.core.schemas import ComplianceChatRequest
                    request_obj = ComplianceChatRequest(question=prompt, question_id="st_session")

                    # Run the full 6-node LangGraph pipeline and get the response
                    response = st.session_state.processor.process_query_with_orchestration(request_obj)

                    if response.needs_clarification:
                        # IntentChecker decided the question is too vague to process
                        st.session_state.messages.append({
                            "role": "notice",
                            "type": "clarify",
                            "content": response.clarification_question,
                        })
                    elif response.error:
                        # A pipeline node returned an error (e.g. max retries exceeded)
                        st.session_state.messages.append({
                            "role": "notice",
                            "type": "error",
                            "content": response.error,
                        })
                    else:
                        # Success - encode result rows as CSV for the download button
                        if response.df_output:
                            df = pd.DataFrame(response.df_output)
                            st.session_state.last_csv = df.to_csv(index=False).encode("utf-8")
                        else:
                            # No rows returned - clear any previous CSV download
                            st.session_state.last_csv = None

                        # Build the metadata block - uses HTML spans so section
                        # labels render at the same 12px monospace as the content,
                        # with no markdown parsing or heading inflation.
                        stages_str = " &gt; ".join(response.stages)
                        sql_safe   = (response.sql_query or "").replace("<", "&lt;").replace(">", "&gt;")
                        data_safe  = json.dumps(response.df_output, indent=2).replace("<", "&lt;").replace(">", "&gt;")
                        meta_content = (
                            f'<span class="cb-label">Agent Trace</span>'
                            f'{stages_str}\n'
                            f'<hr class="cb-divider">'
                            f'<span class="cb-label">Summary</span>'
                            f'{response.result_summary}\n'
                            f'<hr class="cb-divider">'
                            f'<span class="cb-label">SQL</span>'
                            f'{sql_safe}\n'
                            f'<hr class="cb-divider">'
                            f'<span class="cb-label">Data (first rows)</span>'
                            f'{data_safe}'
                        )

                        # Append the full bot response to session history
                        st.session_state.messages.append({
                            "role":    "bot",
                            "content": f"[OK] Query completed {response.result_summary}",
                            "meta":    meta_content,
                            "stages":  response.stages,
                        })

                except Exception as e:
                    # Catch any unexpected error and surface it as a notice card
                    st.session_state.messages.append({
                        "role": "notice",
                        "type": "error",
                        "content": f"Unexpected error: {e!s}",
                    })

            # Force a full re-run so the new message renders immediately
            st.rerun()


if st.session_state.page == "chat":
    with st.sidebar:
        st.divider()
        if st.button("\u2715  New Chat", use_container_width=True):
            # Wipe history and CSV cache, then re-run to show an empty chat
            st.session_state.messages = []
            st.session_state.last_csv = None
            st.rerun()



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\application\launcher.py
####################################################################################################

"""
application/launcher.py — entry point for the Streamlit application.

Starts the Streamlit server bound to the CDSW_APP_PORT environment variable
(default 8551) on localhost.
"""

import os
import subprocess
from pathlib import Path


def run_streamlit() -> None:
    """Launch the Streamlit app using a path derived from this file's location."""
    # Resolve the app path relative to this launcher — no hardcoded absolute paths.
    app_file = str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "app.py")
    address  = "127.0.0.1"
    port     = os.environ.get("CDSW_APP_PORT", "8551")

    command = [
        "streamlit",
        "run",
        app_file,
        f"--server.address={address}",
        f"--server.port={port}",
    ]

    try:
        subprocess.run(command, check=True)
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        print("\nStopping the server...")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_streamlit()

####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\data\_pandas_backend.py
####################################################################################################


import logging
import tempfile
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Literal

import cml.data_v1 as cmldata
import pandas as pd
from sqlglot import Expression, parse_one
from tenacity import (
    before_sleep_log,
    retry,
    retry_if_exception_type,
    stop_after_attempt,
    wait_exponential,
)

from ..utils._hdfs_client import HDFSClient

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


@dataclass
class PandasEngine:
    connection_name: str = "DLH-DEV-UAT - Hive on Tez"

    def __post_init__(self):
        self.hdfs_client = HDFSClient()

    @property
    def cursor(self):
        """Property to get a database cursor for executing queries
        NOTE - This will create a new connection & manually it must be closed after use.
        """
        # TODO - check if this is needed or if we can just use self._create_connection.get_cursor()
        conn = self._create_connection()
        return conn.get_cursor()

    def read_table(self, query: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """Read a Iceberg table on Hive OCP into a Pandas DataFrame using provided sql query

        Parameters
        ----------
        query : str
            SQL query to read the table

        Returns
        -------
        pd.DataFrame
            Pandas DataFrame containing the data from the specified SQL table
        """
        # verifying the read query
        self._verify_sql_query(query)

        with self._create_connection() as base_conn, base_conn.cursor() as cursor:
            logger.debug("Executing query to read the table")
            from impala.util import as_pandas

            cursor.execute(query)
            data = as_pandas(cursor)
            logger.info(f"Successfully read {len(data)} rows")

        # NOTE - reading from 'Hive on Tez' also add table name as prefix to column names,
        # so we remove it here for consistency with other engines
        return data.rename(
            columns={col: col.upper().split(".")[-1] for col in data.columns}
        )

    def read_csv_from_hdfs(self, path: str, **kwargs) -> pd.DataFrame:
        # data = self.hdfs_client.read_csv_file(path)
        # return data.to_table().to_pandas(
        #     split_blocks=True,
        #     self_destruct=True,  # frees Arrow buffers after conversion
        #     zero_copy_only=False,
        # )
        # REVIEW - Ideally pyarrow based code should be used rather than hdfs cli
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
            local_csv_path = f"{temp_dir}/data.csv"
            logger.debug("downloading the csv from hdfs")
            self.hdfs_client.download(path, local_csv_path)
            logger.debug(
                f"successfully downloaded csv temporarily to: {local_csv_path}"
            )
            logger.info(f"loading csv from {local_csv_path}")
            return pd.read_csv(local_csv_path, **kwargs)

    def read_parquet_from_hdfs(self, path: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
        data = self.hdfs_client.read_parquet_table(path)
        return data.to_table().to_pandas(
            split_blocks=True,
            self_destruct=True,  # frees Arrow buffers after conversion
            zero_copy_only=False,
        )

    def save_table(
        self,
        df: pd.DataFrame,
        name: str,
        mode: Literal["overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"] = "append",
        batch_size: int = 5000,
    ):
        """Function to insert data from a Pandas DataFrame into a Iceberg table

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : pd.DataFrame
            table data in the form of pandas dataframe
        name : str
            table name to write/append DEPRECATED -
        mode : Literal["overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"], optional
            how to write data, by default "append"
        """
        if mode != "append":
            raise NotImplementedError(
                f"Mode '{mode}' not yet implemented for Pandas Engine"
            )
        # Prepare Query with Backticks
        # NOTE - splitting by '.' to backtick the schema and table separately: `db`.`table`
        if "." in name:
            db, tbl = name.split(".")
            formatted_table_name = f"`{db}`.`{tbl}`"
        else:
            formatted_table_name = f"`{name}`"

        # Backtick every column name to be safe
        col_names = ", ".join([f"`{c.split('.')[-1]}`" for c in df.columns])
        placeholders = ", ".join(["%s"] * len(df.columns))

        insert_sql_query = (
            f"INSERT INTO {formatted_table_name} ({col_names}) VALUES ({placeholders})"
        )

        no_of_batches = self._calculate_batch_size(df, batch_size)
        logger.info(
            f"Starting to load data into {name} in batches of {batch_size} with total batches: {no_of_batches}"
        )

        with self._create_connection() as base_conn, base_conn.cursor() as cursor:
            try:
                # cursor loop
                for idx, offset in enumerate(
                    range(0, len(df), batch_size), start=1
                ):
                    batch = df.iloc[offset : offset + batch_size]
                    logger.debug(f"inserting batch {idx}: out of {no_of_batches}")
                    cursor.executemany(
                        insert_sql_query, batch.to_dict(orient="records")
                    )
                    logger.debug(
                        f"successfully inserted batch {idx} with {len(batch)} rows."
                    )

                logger.info(f"Successfully loaded {len(df)} rows.")
            except Exception as e:
                logger.error(f"SQL Error: {insert_sql_query}")
                logger.error(f"Details: {e}")
                # FIXME - use precise exception chaining rather than generic. then use it below with `from e`
                # raise from e
                raise  # this is temporary until we fix the above

    def save_csv_to_hdfs(
        self,
        df: pd.DataFrame,
        path: str,
        **kwargs,
    ):
        """Save a pandas DataFrame as a CSV file to HDFS.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : pd.DataFrame
            The DataFrame to save.
        path : str
            The HDFS path where the CSV file will be saved.
        **kwargs : dict
            Additional keyword arguments to pass to `pd.DataFrame.to_csv()`.
        """
        # REVIEW - Check if instead of local copy, we can directly use pyarrow table to write to hdfs
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
            df.to_csv(f"{temp_dir}/data.csv", **kwargs)
            self.hdfs_client.upload(f"{temp_dir}/data.csv", path)
        logger.info(f"saved pandas dataframe to HDFS at {path}")

    def save_parquet(self, df: pd.DataFrame, path: str, **kwargs):
        """Save a pandas DataFrame as a Parquet file to HDFS.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : pd.DataFrame
            The DataFrame to save.
        path : str
            The HDFS path where the Parquet file will be saved.
        **kwargs : dict
            Additional keyword arguments to pass to `pd.DataFrame.to_parquet()`.
        """
        # REVIEW - Check if instead of local copy, we can directly use pyarrow table to write to hdfs
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
            df.to_parquet(f"{temp_dir}/data.parquet", **kwargs)
            self.hdfs_client.upload(f"{temp_dir}/data.parquet", path)
        logger.info(f"saved pandas dataframe to HDFS at {path}")

    @retry(
        stop=stop_after_attempt(5),
        wait=wait_exponential(multiplier=1, max=10),
        retry=retry_if_exception_type(Exception),
        reraise=True,
        before_sleep=before_sleep_log(logger, logging.WARNING),
    )
    def _create_connection(self):
        """Attempt to create and return the DB base connection (with retries)."""
        # TODO - we should explore connection pooling or reuse to avoid the overhead of creating
        # a new connection for every operation, but for now we will create a new connection each
        # time and rely on retries to handle transient issues.

        # if not hasattr(self, "_base_conn") or self._base_conn is None:
        self._base_conn = cmldata.get_connection(self.connection_name)
        logger.info(f"Successfully established connection: {self.connection_name}")
        return self._base_conn.get_base_connection()

    def _calculate_batch_size(self, df: pd.DataFrame, batch_size) -> int:
        """Calculate total bactches based on batch size & data length."""
        # Simple heuristic: 5000 rows per batch as a starting point
        return len(df) // batch_size + (1 if len(df) % batch_size > 0 else 0)

    # TODO - Explore optimizing connection reuse or pooling using SQLAlchemy + Impala

    def _verify_sql_query(self, query: str) -> Expression:
        return parse_one(query)



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\data\_polars_backend.py
####################################################################################################


import logging
import tempfile
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Literal

import cml.data_v1 as cmldata
import polars as pl
from sqlglot import Expression, parse_one
from tenacity import (
    before_sleep_log,
    retry,
    retry_if_exception_type,
    stop_after_attempt,
    wait_exponential,
)

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


@dataclass
class PolarsEngine:
    connection_name: str = "DLH-DEV-UAT - Hive on Tez"

    @property
    def cursor(self):
        """Property to get a database cursor for executing queries
        NOTE - This will create a new connection & manually it must be closed after use.
        """
        # TODO - check if this is needed or if we can just use self._create_connection.get_cursor()
        conn = self._create_connection()
        return conn.get_cursor()

    def read_table(self, query: str, schema_overrides: dict) -> pl.DataFrame:
        """Read a SQL table into a Polars DataFrame using the provided connection and table name

        Parameters
        ----------
        name : str
            name of the SQL table to read, can include schema (e.g. "schema.table

        Returns
        -------
        pl.DataFrame
            Polars DataFrame containing the data from the specified SQL table
        """
        # verifying the read query
        self._verify_sql_query(query)

        with self._create_connection() as base_conn, base_conn.cursor() as cursor:
            logger.debug("Executing query to read the table")
            data = pl.read_database(
                query, connection=cursor, schema_overrides=schema_overrides
            )
            logger.info(f"Successfully read {len(data)} rows")

        # NOTE - reading from 'Hive on Tez' also add table name as prefix to column names,
        # so we remove it here for consistency with other engines
        return data.rename({col: col.upper().split(".")[-1] for col in data.columns})

    def read_csv(self, path: str) -> pl.DataFrame:
        # TODO - TO implement it use boto3 S3 route
        raise NotImplementedError(
            "Support for reading file format not yet implemented in Polars"
        )

    def read_parquet(self, path: str) -> pl.DataFrame:
        # TODO - TO implement it use boto3 S3 route
        raise NotImplementedError(
            "Support for reading file format not yet implemented in Polars"
        )

    def save_table(
        self,
        df: pl.DataFrame,
        name: str,
        mode: Literal["overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"] = "append",
        batch_size: int = 5000,
    ):
        """Function to insert data from a Polars DataFrame into a SQL table

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : pl.DataFrame
            table data in the form of polars dataframe
        name : str
            table name to write/append DEPRECATED -
        mode : Literal["overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"], optional
            how to write data, by default "append"
        """
        if mode != "append":
            raise NotImplementedError(
                f"Mode '{mode}' not yet implemented for Polars Engine"
            )
        # Prepare Query with Backticks
        # NOTE - splitting by '.' to backtick the schema and table separately: `db`.`table`
        if "." in name:
            db, tbl = name.split(".")
            formatted_table_name = f"`{db}`.`{tbl}`"
        else:
            formatted_table_name = f"`{name}`"

        # Backtick every column name to be safe
        col_names = ", ".join([f"`{c.split('.')[-1]}`" for c in df.columns])
        placeholders = ", ".join(["%s"] * len(df.columns))

        insert_sql_query = (
            f"INSERT INTO {formatted_table_name} ({col_names}) VALUES ({placeholders})"
        )

        no_of_batches = self._calculate_batch_size(df, batch_size)
        logger.info(
            f"Starting to load data into {name} in batches of {batch_size} with total batches: {no_of_batches}"
        )

        with self._create_connection() as base_conn, base_conn.cursor() as cursor:
            try:
                # cursor loop
                for idx, offset in enumerate(
                    range(0, len(df), batch_size), start=1
                ):
                    batch = df.slice(offset, batch_size)
                    logger.debug(f"inserting batch {idx}: out of {no_of_batches}")
                    cursor.executemany(insert_sql_query, batch.to_dicts())
                    logger.debug(
                        f"successfully inserted batch {idx} with {len(batch)} rows."
                    )

                logger.info(f"Successfully loaded {len(df)} rows.")
            except Exception as e:
                logger.error(f"SQL Error: {insert_sql_query}")
                logger.error(f"Details: {e}")
                # FIXME - use precise exception chaining rather than generic. then use it below with `from e`
                # raise from e
                raise  # this is temporary until we fix the above

    def save_csv_to_hdfs(
        self,
        df: pl.DataFrame,
        path: str,
        **kwargs,
    ):
        """Save a Polars DataFrame as a CSV file to HDFS.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : pl.DataFrame
            The DataFrame to save.
        path : str
            The HDFS path where the CSV file will be saved.
        **kwargs : dict
            Additional keyword arguments to pass to `pd.DataFrame.to_csv()`.
        """
        # REVIEW - Check if instead of local copy, we can directly use pyarrow table to write to hdfs
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
            df.write_csv(f"{temp_dir}/data.csv", **kwargs)
            self.upload(f"{temp_dir}/data.csv", path)
        logger.info(f"saved polars dataframe to HDFS at {path}")

    def save_parquet_to_hdfs(self, df: pl.DataFrame, path: str, **kwargs):
        """Save a Polars DataFrame as a Parquet file to HDFS.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : pl.DataFrame
            The DataFrame to save.
        path : str
            The HDFS path where the Parquet file will be saved.
        **kwargs : dict
            Additional keyword arguments to pass to `pl.DataFrame.to_parquet()`.
        """
        # REVIEW - Check if instead of local copy, we can directly use pyarrow table to write to hdfs
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
            df.write_parquet(f"{temp_dir}/data.parquet", **kwargs)
            self.upload(f"{temp_dir}/data.parquet", path)
        logger.info(f"saved polars dataframe to HDFS at {path}")

    @retry(
        stop=stop_after_attempt(5),
        wait=wait_exponential(multiplier=1, max=10),
        retry=retry_if_exception_type(Exception),
        reraise=True,
        before_sleep=before_sleep_log(logger, logging.WARNING),
    )
    def _create_connection(self):
        """Attempt to create and return the DB base connection (with retries)."""
        # TODO - we should explore connection pooling or reuse to avoid the overhead of creating
        # a new connection for every operation, but for now we will create a new connection each
        # time and rely on retries to handle transient issues.

        # if not hasattr(self, "_base_conn") or self._base_conn is None:
        self._base_conn = cmldata.get_connection(self.connection_name)
        logger.info(f"Successfully established connection: {self.connection_name}")
        return self._base_conn.get_base_connection()

    def _calculate_batch_size(self, df: pl.DataFrame, batch_size) -> int:
        """Calculate total bactches based on batch size & data length."""
        # Simple heuristic: 5000 rows per batch as a starting point
        return len(df) // batch_size + (1 if len(df) % batch_size > 0 else 0)

    # TODO - Explore optimizing connection reuse or pooling using SQLAlchemy + Impala

    def _verify_sql_query(self, query: str) -> Expression:
        return parse_one(query)



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\data\_spark_backend.py
####################################################################################################


import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Literal
from zoneinfo import ZoneInfo

from pyspark.sql import SparkSession
from pyspark.sql.dataframe import DataFrame
from sqlglot import parse_one

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


@dataclass
class SparkEngine:
    """Spark-backed data access and persistence engine.

    Parameters
    ----------
    app_name_prefix : str
        Prefix used while constructing the Spark application name during
        session initialization.
    """

    app_name_prefix: str

    def __post_init__(self):
        """Initialize the Spark session for the engine instance.

        Notes
        -----
        The Spark application name is created by combining
        ``app_name_prefix`` with the current timestamp in the
        ``Asia/Kolkata`` timezone.
        """
        # FIXME - Below logic of creating spark session logic will work with
        # Notebook session because it can access default-spark.conf but not
        # with CML Jobs
        logger.debug("creating new spark session")
        sess_name = f"{self.app_name_prefix}-{datetime.now(ZoneInfo('Asia/Kolkata')).isoformat()}"
        self.spark = SparkSession.builder.appName(sess_name).getOrCreate()
        logger.debug(f"successfully created new spark session: {sess_name}")

    def read_table(self, name: str) -> DataFrame:
        """Read a table from the Spark catalog.

        Parameters
        ----------
        name : str
            Fully qualified table name to read.

        Returns
        -------
        DataFrame
            Spark DataFrame loaded from the requested table.
        """
        return self.spark.read.table(name)

    def read_csv(self, path: str) -> DataFrame:
        """Read a CSV dataset from the provided path.

        Parameters
        ----------
        path : str
            Filesystem/Ozone path from which the CSV data should be loaded.

        Returns
        -------
        DataFrame
            Spark DataFrame created from the CSV input.
        """
        return self.spark.read.format("csv").option("path", path).load()

    def read_parquet(self, path: str) -> DataFrame:
        """Read a Parquet dataset from the provided path.

        Parameters
        ----------
        path : str
            Filesystem/Ozone path from which the Parquet data should be loaded.

        Returns
        -------
        DataFrame
            Spark DataFrame created from the Parquet input.
        """
        return self.spark.read.format("parquet").option("path", path).load()

    def save_table(
        self,
        df: DataFrame,
        name: str,
        path: str,
        mode: Literal["overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"] = "append",
    ):
        """Persist a DataFrame as an Iceberg table.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : DataFrame
            Spark DataFrame to be written.
        name : str
            Target table name for persistence.
        path : str
            Storage path associated with the table.
        mode : {"overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"}, default="append"
            Write mode to use while saving the table.
        """
        # TODO - extract table path automatically
        df.write.mode(mode).format("iceberg").option("path", path).saveAsTable(name)

    def merge_table(self, df: DataFrame, merge_query: str):
        """Merge a DataFrame into a target Iceberg table using a SQL MERGE statement.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : DataFrame
            Source Spark DataFrame that will be exposed as a temporary view for
            the merge operation.
        merge_query : str
            SQL MERGE statement used to perform the upsert or merge logic.
        """
        # parsing merge query to validate syntax & extract source name for temp view
        parsed_query = parse_one(merge_query)
        source: str = parsed_query.args.get("using").name

        # creating temp view with source table name provided in merge query
        df.createOrReplaceTempView(source)
        logger.debug(f"created {source} temp view")

        self.spark.sql(merge_query)
        logger.info(
            f"successfully merged into {parsed_query.this.db}.{parsed_query.this.name}"
        )

    def save_csv(
        self,
        df: DataFrame,
        path: str,
        mode: Literal["overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"] = "append",
    ):
        """Write a DataFrame to CSV format.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : DataFrame
            Spark DataFrame to be written.
        path : str
            Destination filesystem path for the CSV output.
        mode : {"overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"}, default="append"
            Write mode to use while saving the CSV data.
        """
        df.write.mode(mode).format("csv").save(path)

    def save_parquet(
        self,
        df: DataFrame,
        path: str,
        mode: Literal["overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"] = "append",
    ):
        """Write a DataFrame to Parquet format.

        Parameters
        ----------
        df : DataFrame
            Spark DataFrame to be written.
        path : str
            Destination filesystem path for the Parquet output.
        mode : {"overwrite", "append", "ignore", "error"}, default="append"
            Write mode to use while saving the Parquet data.
        """
        df.write.mode(mode).format("parquet").save(path)

    def close_spark_session(self):
        """Closes curent active spark session"""
        self.spark.stop()

    "def _rand_date(start: date, end: date) -> date:\n",
    "    return start + timedelta(days=random.randint(0, (end - start).days))\n",
    "\n",
    "\n",
    "def _fmt(d: date) -> str:\n",
    "    \"\"\"ISO-8601 string — Hive/Impala accepts DATE literals as 'YYYY-MM-DD'.\"\"\"\n",
    "    return d.strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d\")\n",
    "\n",
    "\n",
    "def _make_row(i: int) -> dict:\n",
    "    \"\"\"Build one row dict whose keys match COMP_TBL column names exactly.\"\"\"\n",
    "    circle_name, circle_cd = random.choice(_CIRCLES)\n",
    "    branch_name            = random.choice(_BRANCHES[circle_cd])\n",
    "    prdct_id, product_name = random.choice(_PRODUCTS)\n",
    "\n",
    "    age: int     = random.randint(18, 85)\n",
    "    sr_flag: int = 1 if age >= 60 else 0\n",
    "    dob: str     = _fmt(_rand_date(date(2025 - age - 1, 1, 1), date(2025 - age, 12, 31)))\n",
    "\n",
    "    kyc: str       = \"Y\" if random.random() < (0.80 if sr_flag else 0.90) else \"N\"\n",
    "    balance: float = round(random.lognormvariate(11.5, 1.2), 2)\n",
    "    balance        = max(10_000.0, min(5_000_000.0, balance))\n",
    "\n",
    "    open_dt: date  = _rand_date(date(2005, 1, 1), date(2024, 6, 30))\n",
    "    tenor_yr: int  = random.choice([1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 10, 15])\n",
    "    tenor_day: int = random.randint(0, 364)\n",
    "    mat_dt_d: date = open_dt + timedelta(days=tenor_yr * 365 + tenor_day)\n",
    "\n",
    "    is_mod: str  = \"YES\" if random.random() < 0.15 else \"NO\"\n",
    "    mtrty_dt: str = (\n",
    "        _fmt(mat_dt_d + timedelta(days=random.randint(-30, 30)))\n",
    "        if is_mod == \"YES\" else _fmt(mat_dt_d)\n",
    "    )\n",
    "\n",
    "    base_rate: float = 7.0 if sr_flag else 6.5\n",
    "    new_rate: float  = round(base_rate + random.uniform(-0.75, 0.75), 6)\n",
    "    exp_rate: float  = round(base_rate + 0.25 + random.uniform(-0.75, 0.75), 6)\n",
    "    pf_fac: str      = \"Y\" if random.random() < 0.30 else \"N\"\n",
    "    brcd: str        = f\"{random.randint(1, 999):03d}\"\n",
    "    acct_brnch: str  = f\"M{random.randint(1, 99):03d}\"\n",
    "\n",
    "    return {\n",
    "        # --- STRING columns --------------------------------------------------\n",
    "        \"BRCD\":               brcd,\n",
    "        \"CUST_NBR\":           f\"C{i:06d}\",\n",
    "        \"CUST_NBR_CHKDGT\":    str(random.randint(1, 9)),\n",
    "        \"ACCT_NBR\":           f\"A{i:06d}\",\n",
    "        \"ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT\":    str(random.randint(1, 9)),\n",
    "        \"ACCT_BRNCH\":         acct_brnch,\n",
    "        # --- DATE columns (string 'YYYY-MM-DD' — Impala/Iceberg accepts it) -\n",
    "        \"BRTH_DT\":            dob,\n",
    "        # --- INT columns -----------------------------------------------------\n",
    "        \"AGE_YRS\":            age,\n",
    "        \"SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG\":   sr_flag,\n",
    "        # --- STRING -----------------------------------------------------------\n",
    "        \"KYC_FLG\":            kyc,\n",
    "        # --- DECIMAL ----------------------------------------------------------\n",
    "        \"END_OF_DAY_BAL\":     round(balance, 2),\n",
    "        # --- DATE columns -----------------------------------------------------\n",
    "        \"ACCT_OPN_DT\":        _fmt(open_dt),\n",
    "        \"MTRTY_DT\":           mtrty_dt,\n",
    "        # --- DECIMAL ----------------------------------------------------------\n",
    "        \"NEW_EFFCTV_RT\":      new_rate,\n",
    "        # --- STRING columns ---------------------------------------------------\n",
    "        \"PRDCT_ID\":           prdct_id,\n",
    "        \"PF_FAC_REPT\":        pf_fac,\n",
    "        \"PRODUCT_NAME\":       product_name,\n",
    "        \"BRANCHNAME\":         branch_name,\n",
    "        \"CIRCLENAME\":         circle_name,\n",
    "        \"CIRCLECD\":           circle_cd,\n",
    "        # --- DECIMAL ----------------------------------------------------------\n",
    "        \"EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT\": exp_rate,\n",
    "        # --- STRING -----------------------------------------------------------\n",
    "        \"ISMOD\":              is_mod,\n",
    "    }\n",
    "\n",
    "\n",
    "rows = [_make_row(i + 1) for i in range(ROW_COUNT)]\n",
    "df   = pd.DataFrame(rows)\n",
    "\n",
    "print(f\"Generated {len(df):,} rows  |  columns: {list(df.columns)}\")\n",
    "df.head(3)"
   ]

    "## Cast columns to Iceberg-compatible dtypes\n",
    "\n",
    "DATE columns must be `object` (string `'YYYY-MM-DD'`) for Impala `INSERT … VALUES`.\n",
    "DECIMAL columns should be Python `float` — Impala coerces them correctly."
   ]
  },
  {
   "cell_type": "code",
   "execution_count": null,
   "id": "cell-03-dtypes",
   "metadata": {
    "id": "cell-03-dtypes"
   },
   "outputs": [],
   "source": [
    "# Cell 3: Dtype coercion — align with Iceberg table schema\n",
    "\n",
    "# STRING -> str (ensure no NaN slips through as float)\n",
    "str_cols = [\n",
    "    \"BRCD\", \"CUST_NBR\", \"CUST_NBR_CHKDGT\", \"ACCT_NBR\", \"ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT\",\n",
    "    \"ACCT_BRNCH\", \"KYC_FLG\", \"PRDCT_ID\", \"PF_FAC_REPT\",\n",
    "    \"PRODUCT_NAME\", \"BRANCHNAME\", \"CIRCLENAME\", \"CIRCLECD\", \"ISMOD\",\n",
    "]\n",
    "for c in str_cols:\n",
    "    df[c] = df[c].astype(str)\n",
    "\n",
    "# DATE -> str 'YYYY-MM-DD' (already strings from generator; keep as object)\n",
    "date_cols = [\"BRTH_DT\", \"ACCT_OPN_DT\", \"MTRTY_DT\"]\n",
    "for c in date_cols:\n",
    "    df[c] = df[c].astype(str)\n",
    "\n",
    "# INT columns\n",
    "df[\"AGE_YRS\"]          = df[\"AGE_YRS\"].astype(int)\n",
    "df[\"SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG\"] = df[\"SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG\"].astype(int)\n",
    "\n",
    "# DECIMAL columns → float64\n",
    "df[\"END_OF_DAY_BAL\"]     = df[\"END_OF_DAY_BAL\"].astype(float)\n",
    "df[\"NEW_EFFCTV_RT\"]      = df[\"NEW_EFFCTV_RT\"].astype(float)\n",
    "df[\"EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT\"] = df[\"EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT\"].astype(float)\n",
    "\n",
    "print(df.dtypes)\n",
    "print(f\"\\nShape: {df.shape}\")"
   ]
  },
  {
   "cell_type": "markdown",
   "id": "05-markdown-push",
   "metadata": {
    "id": "05-markdown-push"
   },
   "source": [
    "## Cell 4 — Push data to Iceberg table `dev_feature_store.COMP_TBL`\n",
    "\n",
    "`PandasEngine.save_table()` batches INSERT statements (default 5 000 rows/batch)\n",
    "via the Hive on Tez CML Data connection.\n",

CREATE TABLE dev_feature_store.COMP_TBL (
    BRCD               STRING COMMENT 'BRANCH CODE OR BRANCH NUMBER',
    CUST_NBR           STRING COMMENT 'CUSTOMER NUMBER — UNIQUE IDENTIFIER',
    CUST_NBR_CHKDGT    STRING COMMENT 'CUSTOMER NUMBER CHECK DIGIT',
    ACCT_NBR           STRING COMMENT 'UNIQUE IDENTIFIER FOR A SPECIFIC BANK ACCOUNT',
    ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT    STRING COMMENT 'ACCOUNT NUMBER CHECK DIGIT',
    ACCT_BRNCH         STRING COMMENT 'ACCOUNT BRANCH',
    BRTH_DT            DATE COMMENT 'BIRTH DATE',
    AGE_YRS            INT COMMENT 'AGE IN YEARS',
    SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG   INT COMMENT 'FLAG: 1 if Senior Citizen, else 0',
    KYC_FLG            STRING COMMENT 'FLAG: Y if KYC complete, else N',
    END_OF_DAY_BAL     DECIMAL(18, 2) COMMENT 'END OF DAY BALANCE',
    ACCT_OPN_DT        DATE COMMENT 'ACCOUNT OPEN DATE',
    MTRTY_DT           DATE COMMENT 'MATURITY DATE',
    NEW_EFFCTV_RT      DECIMAL(10, 6) COMMENT 'NEW EFFECTIVE RATE',
    PRDCT_ID           STRING COMMENT 'PRODUCT ID',
    PF_FAC_REPT        STRING COMMENT 'PENSION FUND FACILITY REPORTING',
    PRODUCT_NAME       STRING COMMENT 'PRODUCT NAME',
    BRANCHNAME         STRING COMMENT 'BRANCH NAME',
    CIRCLENAME         STRING COMMENT 'CIRCLE NAME',
    CIRCLECD           STRING COMMENT 'CIRCLE CODE',
    EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT DECIMAL(10, 6) COMMENT 'EXPECTED RATE OF INTEREST',
    ISMOD              STRING COMMENT 'FLAG: YES if MOD, otherwise NO'
)
STORED BY ICEBERG;


####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\data\seed_synthetic_data.py
####################################################################################################








"""
Synthetic data generator for COMP_TBL.

Creates the database file, creates the table if absent, and inserts
realistic term-deposit compliance records into
``data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db``.  Run from the project root:

    python data/seed_synthetic_data.py              # create + insert 2000 rows
    python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset      # drop all rows then insert
    python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --count 500  # custom row count
    python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --count 0    # create DB/table only, no rows

The script uses only the Python standard library — no external packages needed.

Modular structure
-----------------
  ensure_database(db_path)          → creates the .db file and parent directories
  ensure_table(conn)                → creates COMP_TBL if it does not exist
  reset_table(conn)                 → deletes all existing rows
  generate_rows(count, start_i)     → returns a list of synthetic row tuples
  insert_rows(conn, rows)           → bulk-inserts rows
  print_summary(conn, db_path, n)   → prints statistics after insert
  seed(db_path, count, reset)       → orchestrates all of the above
"""

import argparse
import random
import sqlite3
from datetime import date, timedelta
from pathlib import Path

_CREATE_TABLE_SQL = """
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS COMP_TBL (
    BRCD               TEXT,
    CUST_NBR           TEXT,
    CUST_NBR_CHKDGT    TEXT,
    ACCT_NBR           TEXT,
    ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT    TEXT,
    ACCT_BRNCH         TEXT,
    BRTH_DT            TEXT,
    AGE_YRS            INTEGER,
    SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG   INTEGER,
    KYC_FLG            TEXT,
    END_OF_DAY_BAL     REAL,
    ACCT_OPN_DT        TEXT,
    MTRTY_DT           TEXT,
    tenor_yr           INTEGER,
    tenor_day          INTEGER,
    MAT_DT             TEXT,
    NEW_EFFCTV_RT      REAL,
    PRDCT_ID           TEXT,
    PF_FAC_REPT        TEXT,
    PRODUCT_NAME       TEXT,
    BRANCHNAME         TEXT,
    CIRCLENAME         TEXT,
    CIRCLECD           TEXT,
    EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT REAL,
    ISMOD              TEXT
)
"""

_INSERT_SQL = """
INSERT INTO COMP_TBL (
    BRCD, CUST_NBR, CUST_NBR_CHKDGT, ACCT_NBR, ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT, ACCT_BRNCH,
    BRTH_DT, AGE_YRS, SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG, KYC_FLG, END_OF_DAY_BAL, ACCT_OPN_DT,
    MTRTY_DT, tenor_yr, tenor_day, MAT_DT, NEW_EFFCTV_RT, PRDCT_ID, PF_FAC_REPT,
    PRODUCT_NAME, BRANCHNAME, CIRCLENAME, CIRCLECD, EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT, ISMOD
) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
"""

_REQUIRED_COLUMNS: set[str] = {
    "BRCD", "CUST_NBR", "CUST_NBR_CHKDGT", "ACCT_NBR", "ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT",
    "ACCT_BRNCH", "BRTH_DT", "AGE_YRS", "SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG", "KYC_FLG",
    "END_OF_DAY_BAL", "ACCT_OPN_DT", "MTRTY_DT", "tenor_yr", "tenor_day",
    "MAT_DT", "NEW_EFFCTV_RT", "PRDCT_ID", "PF_FAC_REPT", "PRODUCT_NAME",
    "BRANCHNAME", "CIRCLENAME", "CIRCLECD", "EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT", "ISMOD",
}

def ensure_database(db_path: Path) -> None:
    """
    Create the parent directory and SQLite database file if they do not exist.

    This is a no-op when both already exist — SQLite creates the file on
    first ``connect()`` so the function just guarantees the directory is there.

    Args:
        db_path: Absolute or relative path to the ``.db`` file.
    """
    db_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    existed = db_path.exists()
    sqlite3.connect(str(db_path)).close()
    if not existed:
        print(f"[create] Database file created: {db_path}")
    else:
        print(f"[ok]     Database file exists:  {db_path}")


def ensure_table(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> None:
    """
    Create ``COMP_TBL`` with the full 25-column schema if it does not exist.

    If the table already exists but is missing required columns (stale schema),
    it is dropped and recreated so the seeder can always run safely.

    Args:
        conn: Open SQLite connection to the target database.
    """
    table_exists = conn.execute(
        "SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' AND name='COMP_TBL'"
    ).fetchone()

    if table_exists:
        existing_cols: set[str] = {
            row[1] for row in conn.execute("PRAGMA table_info(COMP_TBL)").fetchall()
        }
        if not _REQUIRED_COLUMNS.issubset(existing_cols):
            conn.execute("DROP TABLE COMP_TBL")
            conn.commit()
            print("[migrate] COMP_TBL schema was stale — dropped and will be recreated.")
            table_exists = None  # fall through to CREATE

    if not table_exists:
        conn.execute(_CREATE_TABLE_SQL)
        conn.commit()
        print("[create] COMP_TBL created.")
    else:
        print("[ok]     COMP_TBL already exists with correct schema.")


def reset_table(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> None:
    """
    Delete every row from ``COMP_TBL``.

    Args:
        conn: Open SQLite connection to the target database.
    """
    deleted: int = conn.execute("DELETE FROM COMP_TBL").rowcount
    conn.commit()
    print(f"[reset]  Deleted {deleted:,} existing rows from COMP_TBL.")


def _rand_date(start: date, end: date) -> date:
    """Return a random date between *start* and *end* (inclusive)."""
    return start + timedelta(days=random.randint(0, (end - start).days))


def _fmt(d: date) -> str:
    """Format a date as ISO-8601 string (YYYY-MM-DD)."""
    return d.strftime("%Y-%m-%d")


def _rand_rate(base: float) -> float:
    """Return a rate within ±0.75 pp of *base*, rounded to 2 dp."""
    return round(base + random.uniform(-0.75, 0.75), 2)


def _make_row(i: int) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    """Generate one synthetic COMP_TBL row."""
    circle_name, circle_cd = random.choice(_CIRCLES)
    branch_name            = random.choice(_BRANCHES[circle_cd])
    prdct_id, product_name = random.choice(_PRODUCTS)

    age: int     = random.randint(18, 85)
    sr_flag: int = 1 if age >= 60 else 0
    dob: str     = _fmt(_rand_date(date(2025 - age - 1, 1, 1), date(2025 - age, 12, 31)))

    kyc: str        = "Y" if random.random() < (0.80 if sr_flag else 0.90) else "N"
    balance: float  = round(random.lognormvariate(11.5, 1.2), 2)
    balance         = max(10_000.0, min(5_000_000.0, balance))

    open_dt: date   = _rand_date(date(2005, 1, 1), date(2024, 6, 30))
    tenor_yr: int   = random.choice([1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 10, 15])
    tenor_day: int  = random.randint(0, 364)
    mat_dt_d: date  = open_dt + timedelta(days=tenor_yr * 365 + tenor_day)
    mat_dt: str     = _fmt(mat_dt_d)

    is_mod: str  = "YES" if random.random() < 0.15 else "NO"
    mtrty_dt: str = _fmt(mat_dt_d + timedelta(days=random.randint(-30, 30))) if is_mod == "YES" else mat_dt

    base_rate: float = 7.0 if sr_flag else 6.5
    new_rate: float  = _rand_rate(base_rate)
    exp_rate: float  = _rand_rate(base_rate + 0.25)
    pf_fac: str      = "Y" if random.random() < 0.30 else "N"
    brcd: str        = f"{random.randint(1, 999):03d}"
    acct_brnch: str  = f"M{random.randint(1, 99):03d}"

    return (
        brcd,                        # BRCD
        f"C{i:06d}",                 # CUST_NBR
        str(random.randint(1, 9)),   # CUST_NBR_CHKDGT
        f"A{i:06d}",                 # ACCT_NBR
        str(random.randint(1, 9)),   # ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT
        acct_brnch,                  # ACCT_BRNCH
        dob,                         # BRTH_DT
        age,                         # AGE_YRS
        sr_flag,                     # SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG
        kyc,                         # KYC_FLG
        balance,                     # END_OF_DAY_BAL
        _fmt(open_dt),               # ACCT_OPN_DT
        mtrty_dt,                    # MTRTY_DT
        tenor_yr,                    # tenor_yr
        tenor_day,                   # tenor_day
        mat_dt,                      # MAT_DT
        new_rate,                    # NEW_EFFCTV_RT
        prdct_id,                    # PRDCT_ID
        pf_fac,                      # PF_FAC_REPT
        product_name,                # PRODUCT_NAME
        branch_name,                 # BRANCHNAME
        circle_name,                 # CIRCLENAME
        circle_cd,                   # CIRCLECD
        exp_rate,                    # EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT
        is_mod,                      # ISMOD
    )


def generate_rows(count: int, start_i: int) -> list[tuple[str, ...]]:
    """
    Generate *count* synthetic row tuples starting from index *start_i*.

    ``start_i`` is used for ``CUST_NBR`` / ``ACCT_NBR`` sequential IDs so
    appending rows never collides with existing ones.

    Args:
        count:   Number of rows to generate.
        start_i: Index of the first new row (1-based).

    Returns:
        List of row tuples ready for ``executemany``.
    """
    return [_make_row(start_i + i) for i in range(count)]


def insert_rows(conn: sqlite3.Connection, rows: list[tuple[str, ...]]) -> None:
    """
    Bulk-insert *rows* into ``COMP_TBL`` using a single ``executemany``.

    Args:
        conn: Open SQLite connection (must already have COMP_TBL).
        rows: Row tuples as returned by :func:`generate_rows`.
    """
    conn.executemany(_INSERT_SQL, rows)
    conn.commit()
    print(f"[insert] {len(rows):,} rows inserted into COMP_TBL.")


def print_summary(conn: sqlite3.Connection, db_path: Path, inserted: int) -> None:
    """
    Print a statistics summary after the insert.

    Args:
        conn:     Open SQLite connection.
        db_path:  Path to the database file (for display only).
        inserted: Number of rows that were just inserted.
    """
    total: int   = conn.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL").fetchone()[0]
    sr: int      = conn.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL WHERE SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG = 1").fetchone()[0]
    kyc_n: int   = conn.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL WHERE KYC_FLG = 'N'").fetchone()[0]
    circles: list[tuple[str, int]] = conn.execute(
        "SELECT CIRCLENAME, COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL GROUP BY CIRCLENAME ORDER BY 2 DESC LIMIT 5"
    ).fetchall()

    print(f"\n{'='*55}")
    print(f"  Database : {db_path}")
    print(f"  Inserted : {inserted:,} rows")
    print(f"  Total    : {total:,} rows in COMP_TBL")
    print(f"{'='*55}")
    print(f"  Senior citizens  (SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG=1) : {sr:,}")
    print(f"  KYC non-compliant (KYC_FLG='N')        : {kyc_n:,}")
    print("\n  Top 5 circles by row count:")
    for name, cnt in circles:
        print(f"    {name:<22} {cnt:>5}")
    print(f"{'='*55}\n")


def seed(db_path: Path, count: int, reset: bool) -> None:
    """
    Full end-to-end pipeline:

    1. :func:`ensure_database` — create ``.db`` file and parent dirs if absent.
    2. :func:`ensure_table`    — create ``COMP_TBL`` if absent or stale.
    3. :func:`reset_table`     — (only when ``reset=True``) delete all rows.
    4. :func:`generate_rows`   — build synthetic row tuples in memory.
    5. :func:`insert_rows`     — bulk-insert into the database.
    6. :func:`print_summary`   — display post-insert statistics.

    Args:
        db_path: Path to the SQLite ``.db`` file.
        count:   Number of rows to insert (0 = create DB/table only).
        reset:   When ``True``, delete all existing rows before inserting.
    """
    ensure_database(db_path)

    conn = sqlite3.connect(str(db_path))

    try:
        ensure_table(conn)

        if reset:
            reset_table(conn)

        if count == 0:
            print("[info]   --count 0: skipping row insert.")
            print_summary(conn, db_path, inserted=0)
            return

        start_i: int = conn.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL").fetchone()[0] + 1
        rows = generate_rows(count, start_i)

        insert_rows(conn, rows)

        print_summary(conn, db_path, inserted=count)

    finally:
        conn.close()


def _parse_args() -> argparse.Namespace:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description=(
            "Create the SQLite database + COMP_TBL schema and insert synthetic data.\n"
            "Safe to re-run: creates only what is missing."
        ),
        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,
    )
    p.add_argument(
        "--count", type=int, default=2000,
        help="Number of rows to insert (default: 2000). Use 0 to create DB/table only.",
    )
    p.add_argument(
        "--reset", action="store_true",
        help="Delete all existing rows before inserting.",
    )
    p.add_argument(
        "--db", type=Path,
        default=Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "comp_db" / "COMP_REPORTS.db",
        help="Path to the SQLite database file.",
    )
    p.add_argument(
        "--seed", type=int, default=42,
        help="Random seed for reproducibility (default: 42).",
    )
    return p.parse_args()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    args = _parse_args()
    random.seed(args.seed)
    seed(db_path=args.db, count=args.count, reset=args.reset)

CREATE TABLE "COMP_TBL" (
    "CUST_NBR" FLOAT,
    "CUST_NBR_CHKDGT" FLOAT,
    "ACCT_NBR" FLOAT,
    "ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT" FLOAT,
    "ACCT_BRNCH" FLOAT,
    "BRTH_DT" DATETIME,
    "AGE_YRS" FLOAT,
    "SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG" FLOAT,
    "KYC_FLG" TEXT,
    "END_OF_DAY_BAL" FLOAT,
    "ACCT_OPN_DT" DATETIME,
    "MTRTY_DT" DATETIME,
    "tenor_yr" BIGINT,
    "tenor_day" BIGINT,
    "MAT_DT" DATETIME,
    "NEW_EFFCTV_RT" FLOAT,
    "PRDCT_ID" FLOAT,
    "PF_FAC_REPT" TEXT,
    "PRODUCT_NAME" TEXT,
    "BRANCHNAME" TEXT,
    "CIRCLENAME" TEXT,
    "CIRCLECD" FLOAT,
    "EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT" FLOAT,
    "ISMOD" TEXT
);
