####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\genai\core\exceptions.py
####################################################################################################

"""
genai/core/exceptions.py — domain exception hierarchy for the Compliance Chatbot.

All application-specific exceptions inherit from ``ComplianceBotError`` so
callers can catch them at a single abstraction level.  Internal stack traces
are never leaked to the UI.

Design principle (Chollet)
--------------------------
Every exception carries three fields that tell the user:
  - ``what``     — what happened (the error)
  - ``expected`` — what was expected (the valid state)
  - ``fix``      — how to resolve it

This makes error messages self-explanatory:

    You provided an empty question.
    Expected: a natural-language compliance question, e.g. 'Show accounts with KYC=N'.
    Fix: type a question in the chat input and submit again.
"""


# BASE EXCEPTION

class ComplianceBotError(Exception):
    """
    Base exception for all Compliance Chatbot domain errors.

    Parameters
    ----------
    message : str
        Human-readable error message shown to the user or in logs.
    detail : str | None
        Optional technical detail — used for logging, never shown in the UI.
    what : str | None
        What happened (overrides message in structured output).
    expected : str | None
        What the system expected instead.
    fix : str | None
        Actionable suggestion for the user.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        message:  str,
        detail:   str | None = None,
        what:     str | None = None,
        expected: str | None = None,
        fix:      str | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__(message)
        self.message:  str       = message
        self.detail:   str | None = detail
        self.what:     str | None = what or message
        self.expected: str | None = expected
        self.fix:      str | None = fix

    def user_message(self) -> str:
        """
        Compose the structured user-facing message:
        ``What happened. Expected: … Fix: …``

        Only the non-None sections are included.
        """
        parts = [self.what or self.message]
        if self.expected:
            parts.append(f"Expected: {self.expected}")
        if self.fix:
            parts.append(f"Fix: {self.fix}")
        return "\n".join(parts)

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        return self.message


# LLM / MODEL ERRORS

class ModelLoadError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when the LLM client fails to initialise (bad URL, auth, etc.)."""


class ModelInferenceError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when the model returns an unexpected response during inference."""


class LLMProviderError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when an LLM provider HTTP call fails (auth, 4xx/5xx, bad body)."""


class LLMTimeoutError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when an LLM provider call exceeds the configured timeout."""


class UnknownProviderError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when an unrecognised provider name is passed to ProviderManager."""


# SQL GENERATION / EXECUTION ERRORS

class SqlGenerationError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when the pipeline cannot produce a valid SQL statement after retries."""


class SqlExecutionError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when the generated SQL fails to execute against the Iceberg table."""


# DATABASE / CONNECTION ERRORS

class DatabaseConnectionError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when the application cannot connect to the configured Iceberg / Hive endpoint."""


class RecordNotFoundError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when an expected database record is absent."""


# DATA PIPELINE ERRORS

class DataIngestionError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when raw data cannot be ingested or structured correctly."""


class SchemaValidationError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when an incoming payload fails Pydantic schema validation."""



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\genai\core\model.py
####################################################################################################




"""
genai/core/model.py — LLM provider lifecycle, inference, and management.

All LLM-related code lives here; nothing else in genai/core/ touches the
OpenAI client directly.
"""

import re
import time

import httpx
from openai import APIError, APITimeoutError, OpenAI

from genai.core.exceptions import (
    ModelInferenceError,
    ModelLoadError,
    UnknownProviderError,
)
from utils.config import (
    DEFAULT_MAX_NEW_TOKENS,
    DEFAULT_TEMPERATURE,
    SQL_BEGIN_TAG,
    SQL_END_TAG,
    ModelSettings,
    get_settings,
)
from utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.core.model")


# ModelLoaderService

class ModelLoaderService:
    """Manages the lifecycle of the configured remote LLM provider."""

    def __init__(self, settings: ModelSettings) -> None:
        # Store the settings so they can be inspected after construction
        self.settings: ModelSettings = settings
        # The OpenAI client — None until load() is called
        self._client: OpenAI | None = None
        # Always send temperature/max_tokens to the DLH endpoint
        self._send_sampling_params = True

    def load(self) -> None:
        """Initialise the provider client.  Call this before any other method."""
        # Currently only one provider is supported; the method name is kept
        # generic so a future provider can be added with minimal changes.
        self._load_dlh_client()

    def _load_dlh_client(self) -> None:
        """Create the DLH OpenAI-compatible HTTP client."""
        http_client = None

        # When SSL verification is disabled (e.g. dev/self-signed certs),
        # create an httpx.Client with verify=False and pass it to OpenAI.
        if not self.settings.dlh_verify_ssl:
            http_client = httpx.Client(verify=False)

        try:
            self._client = OpenAI(
                api_key=self.settings.dlh_api_key,
                base_url=self.settings.dlh_base_url,
                timeout=30.0,  # 30-second total timeout per request
                # Only inject http_client kwarg when we actually have one
                **({"http_client": http_client} if http_client is not None else {}),
            )
            LOGGER.info("DLH client initialised successfully.")
        except Exception as exc:  # pragma: no cover
            # Wrap any init error in our domain exception
            raise ModelLoadError(f"Failed to initialise DLH client: {exc}", detail=str(exc)) from exc

    def is_loaded(self) -> bool:
        """Return True when the client is initialised and ready for inference."""
        return self._client is not None

    def test_connection(self) -> None:
        """Send a minimal request to verify the LLM endpoint is reachable."""
        if self._client is None:
            raise ModelLoadError("Remote client is not initialised.")

        # Use the smallest possible request to check auth and connectivity
        request_kwargs = {
            "model":    self.settings.model_name,
            "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "Reply with OK."}],
            "temperature": 0.1,
            "max_tokens":  8,  # just enough for a "OK" response
        }

        try:
            # We only care that this doesn't raise — the response is ignored
            self._client.chat.completions.create(**request_kwargs)
        except APITimeoutError as exc:
            raise ModelInferenceError(f"Connection test timed out: {exc}", detail=str(exc)) from exc
        except APIError as exc:
            raise ModelInferenceError(f"Connection test failed: {exc}", detail=str(exc)) from exc

    def generate(
        self,
        prompt: str,
        max_new_tokens: int = DEFAULT_MAX_NEW_TOKENS,
        temperature: float  = DEFAULT_TEMPERATURE,
    ) -> str:
        """Send the prompt to the LLM and return the raw text response."""
        # Guard — caller must call load() before generate()
        if not self.is_loaded():
            raise ModelInferenceError(
                "Model is not loaded. Call ModelLoaderService.load() before generating."
            )

        # Build the chat-completions request body
        request_kwargs = {
            "model":    self.settings.model_name,
            "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": prompt}],
            "temperature": temperature,
            "max_tokens":  max_new_tokens,
        }

        try:
            response = self._client.chat.completions.create(**request_kwargs)
            # Return the first (and only) completion; fall back to "" if None
            return response.choices[0].message.content or ""
        except APITimeoutError as exc:
            raise ModelInferenceError(f"Provider request timed out: {exc}", detail=str(exc)) from exc
        except APIError as exc:
            raise ModelInferenceError(f"Provider API error: {exc}", detail=str(exc)) from exc

    def extract_sql(self, model_output: str) -> str | None:
        """
        Pull the SQL statement out of the LLM's raw output.

        The model is instructed to wrap its SQL between <begin_sql_response>
        and <end_sql_response> tags.  This method finds that section and
        returns the trimmed SQL string, or None if the tags are missing.
        """
        # Build a regex that matches everything between the two delimiter tags
        pattern = re.compile(
            rf"{re.escape(SQL_BEGIN_TAG)}\s*(.*?)\s*{re.escape(SQL_END_TAG)}",
            re.DOTALL | re.IGNORECASE,
        )
        match = pattern.search(model_output)
        if match:
            sql = match.group(1).strip()
            LOGGER.debug("Extracted SQL: %s", sql)
            return sql

        # No tags found — log a warning with the first 300 chars for debugging
        LOGGER.warning("SQL delimiter tags not found in model output. Raw: %s", model_output[:300])
        return None

    def unload(self) -> None:
        """Release the client from memory — useful when swapping providers."""
        LOGGER.info("Unloading model resources …")
        self._client = None  # allow garbage collection
        LOGGER.info("Model unloaded.")



def create_model_service(settings: "ModelSettings | None" = None) -> ModelLoaderService:
    """
    Build a ModelLoaderService, call load(), and return it ready for inference.

    Pass a ModelSettings object to override defaults, or leave as None to
    use whatever is configured in .env / AppSettings.
    """
    # Fall back to AppSettings when no override is provided
    resolved = settings or get_settings().model
    LOGGER.info(
        "create_model_service — provider=%s model=%s",
        resolved.provider, resolved.model_name,
    )
    service = ModelLoaderService(settings=resolved)
    service.load()  # initialises the HTTP client
    return service


def generate_with_retry(
    model_service: ModelLoaderService,
    prompt:         str,
    max_new_tokens: int   = DEFAULT_MAX_NEW_TOKENS,
    temperature:    float = DEFAULT_TEMPERATURE,
    retries:        int   = 2,
    retry_delay:    float = 1.0,
) -> str:
    """
    Call model_service.generate() with automatic retry on failure.

    retries      — extra attempts after the first (total = retries + 1).
    retry_delay  — seconds to wait between attempts.
    Raises ModelInferenceError if every attempt fails.
    """
    last_exc: ModelInferenceError | None = None

    for attempt in range(retries + 1):
        try:
            # Attempt inference — return immediately on success
            return model_service.generate(
                prompt=prompt,
                max_new_tokens=max_new_tokens,
                temperature=temperature,
            )
        except ModelInferenceError as exc:
            last_exc = exc
            if attempt < retries:
                # Log the failure and wait before the next attempt
                LOGGER.warning(
                    "generate_with_retry — attempt %d/%d failed: %s. Retrying in %.1fs…",
                    attempt + 1, retries + 1, exc, retry_delay,
                )
                time.sleep(retry_delay)

    # All attempts exhausted — re-raise the last exception
    raise last_exc  # type: ignore[misc]


def extract_text_between(text: str, start_marker: str, end_marker: str) -> str | None:
    """
    Return the substring between start_marker and end_marker (case-insensitive).

    Returns None when either marker is missing from the text.
    """
    lower = text.lower()

    # Find the start marker position (case-insensitive)
    start_idx = lower.find(start_marker.lower())
    if start_idx == -1:
        return None  # start marker not found

    # Content starts right after the marker
    content_start = start_idx + len(start_marker)

    # Find the end marker, starting the search after the content start
    end_idx = lower.find(end_marker.lower(), content_start)
    if end_idx == -1:
        return None  # end marker not found

    return text[content_start:end_idx].strip()


def truncate_prompt(prompt: str, max_chars: int = 8000) -> str:
    """
    Keep the prompt under max_chars by removing characters from the middle.

    Keeping the head (system role + rules) and tail (question) gives the LLM
    the most important context even when the schema block is very long.
    """
    if len(prompt) <= max_chars:
        return prompt  # already short enough — nothing to do

    # Split the budget evenly between head and tail
    half = max_chars // 2
    LOGGER.warning("truncate_prompt — length %d > %d; cutting the middle.", len(prompt), max_chars)
    return prompt[:half] + "\n...[truncated]...\n" + prompt[-half:]


# ProviderManager

class ProviderManager:
    """
    Registry and lifecycle manager for multiple LLM provider services.

    Register providers by name, then activate one to make it live.
    All inference calls are routed through the currently active provider.
    """

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        # Maps provider name → ModelSettings (config only, not loaded yet)
        self._registry: dict[str, ModelSettings] = {}
        # Maps provider name → loaded ModelLoaderService (loaded on demand)
        self._services: dict[str, ModelLoaderService] = {}
        # Name of the currently active provider, or None
        self._active_name: str | None = None

    def register(self, name: str, settings: ModelSettings) -> None:
        """Store a provider configuration under name (not loaded until activate())."""
        self._registry[name] = settings
        LOGGER.info("ProviderManager — registered provider: %s", name)

    def activate(self, name: str) -> ModelLoaderService:
        """Make name the active provider, loading its client if not yet loaded."""
        if name not in self._registry:
            # Can't activate something that was never registered
            raise UnknownProviderError(
                f"Provider '{name}' is not registered. Available: {list(self._registry)}"
            )
        if name not in self._services:
            # First activation — build and load the service
            service = ModelLoaderService(settings=self._registry[name])
            service.load()
            self._services[name] = service
            LOGGER.info("ProviderManager — loaded provider: %s", name)

        # Switch the active pointer to the requested provider
        self._active_name = name
        LOGGER.info("ProviderManager — active provider: %s", name)
        return self._services[name]

    @property
    def active(self) -> ModelLoaderService:
        """Return the currently active service.  Raises RuntimeError if none is set."""
        if self._active_name is None or self._active_name not in self._services:
            raise RuntimeError("No active provider. Call ProviderManager.activate(name) first.")
        return self._services[self._active_name]

    @property
    def active_name(self) -> str | None:
        """Name of the currently active provider, or None if none has been activated."""
        return self._active_name

    def registered_names(self) -> list[str]:
        """Return a sorted list of all registered provider names."""
        return sorted(self._registry)

    def unload(self, name: str) -> None:
        """Unload the named provider service and free its memory."""
        service = self._services.pop(name, None)
        if service is not None:
            service.unload()  # releases the HTTP client
            LOGGER.info("ProviderManager — unloaded provider: %s", name)
        # Clear the active pointer if the unloaded provider was active
        if self._active_name == name:
            self._active_name = None

    def unload_all(self) -> None:
        """Unload every registered provider service — call on application shutdown."""
        for name in list(self._services):
            self.unload(name)



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\genai\core\processor.py
####################################################################################################

"""
genai/core/processor.py — compliance orchestration layer.

``ComplianceProcessor`` is the single entry-point between the Streamlit UI (or
``CompliancePipeline``) and the compiled LangGraph graph.  It accepts a
``ComplianceChatRequest``, invokes the graph, and maps the resulting
``AgentState`` onto the Pydantic response models.

Intentionally thin — all multi-agent logic lives inside the agent nodes and
graph wiring.  This class only maps data shapes.
"""

import uuid
from typing import Any

from genai.core.exceptions import ComplianceBotError
from genai.core.schemas import (
    ComplianceChatRequest,
    ComplianceChatResponse,
    ComplianceOrchestrationResponse,
    build_error_response,
)
from genai.nl2sql_agent.state import AgentState
from utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.core.processor")


class ComplianceProcessor:
    """
    Orchestrates the NL2SQL compliance query pipeline via LangGraph.

    Parameters
    ----------
    compiled_graph : CompiledStateGraph
        A compiled LangGraph StateGraph returned by ``build_nl2sql_graph()``
        or ``create_graph()``.

    Usage
    -----
    Prefer ``CompliancePipeline`` for new code — it wraps this class and
    hides graph construction.  Use ``ComplianceProcessor`` directly only
    when you need to inject a pre-built graph (e.g. in tests).
    """

    def __init__(self, compiled_graph: Any) -> None:
        self._graph = compiled_graph

    def process_query(self, request: ComplianceChatRequest) -> ComplianceChatResponse:
        """Run the pipeline and return the simplified chat response (no stage trail)."""
        orchestration = self.process_query_with_orchestration(request)
        return ComplianceChatResponse(
            question_id    = orchestration.question_id,
            sql_query      = orchestration.sql_query,
            df_output      = orchestration.df_output,
            result_columns = orchestration.result_columns,
            result_summary = orchestration.result_summary,
            error          = orchestration.error or orchestration.clarification_question,
        )

    def process_query_with_orchestration(
        self,
        request: ComplianceChatRequest,
    ) -> ComplianceOrchestrationResponse:
        """Run the full LangGraph pipeline and return the detailed response."""
        question_id = request.question_id or str(uuid.uuid4())
        question    = (request.question or "").strip()

        if not question:
            LOGGER.warning("[%s] Empty question received.", question_id)
            return build_error_response(
                question_id   = question_id,
                error_message = (
                    "You did not provide a question.\n"
                    "Expected: a natural-language compliance query, "
                    "e.g. 'Show accounts where KYC flag is N'.\n"
                    "Fix: type your question in the chat input and submit again."
                ),
                stage_tag     = "validation:empty_question",
            )

        LOGGER.info("[%s] Invoking NL2SQL graph for: %r", question_id, question)

        initial_state: AgentState = AgentState(  # type: ignore[misc]
            question    = question,
            question_id = question_id,
            stages      = [],
        )

        try:
            final_state: AgentState = self._graph.invoke(initial_state)
        except ComplianceBotError:
            raise
        except Exception:
            LOGGER.exception("[%s] Unexpected graph error", question_id)
            return build_error_response(
                question_id   = question_id,
                error_message = (
                    "An unexpected error occurred in the pipeline.\n"
                    "Expected: the query to complete successfully.\n"
                    "Fix: check the server logs for a full traceback, "
                    "or rephrase your question and try again."
                ),
                stage_tag     = "graph:unexpected_error",
            )

        return _map_state_to_response(question_id, final_state)


def _map_state_to_response(
    question_id: str,
    state: AgentState,
) -> ComplianceOrchestrationResponse:
    """Convert the final AgentState dict into a ComplianceOrchestrationResponse."""
    clarification  = state.get("clarification_needed")
    sql_query      = state.get("sql_query") or None
    result_rows    = state.get("result_rows") or None
    result_columns = list(state.get("result_columns") or [])
    result_summary = state.get("result_summary") or None
    error          = state.get("error") or None
    stages         = list(state.get("stages", []))
    interpreted    = state.get("interpreted_question") or state.get("question", "")

    LOGGER.info(
        "[%s] Pipeline complete — stages=%s rows=%s error=%s",
        question_id, stages, len(result_rows) if result_rows else 0, error,
    )

    return ComplianceOrchestrationResponse(
        question_id            = question_id,
        interpreted_question   = interpreted,
        needs_clarification    = bool(clarification),
        clarification_question = clarification,
        sql_query              = sql_query,
        df_output              = result_rows,
        result_columns         = result_columns,
        result_summary         = result_summary,
        stages                 = stages,
        error                  = error,
    )



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\genai\core\schemas.py
####################################################################################################




"""
genai/core/schemas.py — Pydantic request/response models and factory helpers.

These models are the single source of truth for the data contract between
the Streamlit UI, the ComplianceProcessor, and the LangGraph pipeline.
Factory functions (build_*) construct the models from individual outputs so
callers never repeat the same keyword arguments.
"""

from typing import Any

from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, field_validator

# REQUEST MODELS

class ComplianceChatRequest(BaseModel):
    """Payload sent by the Streamlit UI to the processor for every query."""

    # Optional caller-supplied key — auto-generated as UUID if not provided
    question_id: str | None = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Optional caller-supplied idempotency key.",
        examples=["req-001"],
    )
    # The natural-language compliance question from the user
    question: str | None = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Natural-language compliance question.",
        examples=["Show customers whose age is more than 60 and KYC flag is Y."],
    )

    @field_validator("question")
    @classmethod
    def question_must_not_be_blank(cls, value: str | None) -> str | None:
        """Reject blank strings; strip leading/trailing whitespace from valid values."""
        # A non-None all-whitespace string is semantically empty — reject it
        if value is not None and not value.strip():
            raise ValueError("'question' must not be a blank string.")
        # Strip whitespace from valid non-null values before they reach the pipeline
        return value.strip() if value else value


class LLMConnectionRequest(BaseModel):
    """Payload for testing a new DLH LLM connection from the config page."""

    # Provider is always "dlh" for this on-prem deployment
    provider:       str       = Field(default="dlh", description="LLM provider (dlh).")
    model_name:     str | None = Field(default=None)
    dlh_base_url:   str | None = Field(default=None)
    dlh_api_key:    str | None = Field(default=None)
    dlh_verify_ssl: bool       = Field(default=False)


# RESPONSE MODELS

class ComplianceChatResponse(BaseModel):
    """Simplified response for the basic chat path (no stage audit trail)."""

    question_id:    str | None                    = Field(default=None)
    sql_query:      str | None                    = Field(default=None)
    # Result rows as a list of column→value dicts (None if no rows)
    df_output:      list[dict[str, object]] | None = Field(default=None)
    # Ordered list of column names from the SELECT result
    result_columns: list[str]                     = Field(default_factory=list)
    result_summary: str | None                    = Field(default=None)
    # Non-None when the pipeline encountered an error or needs clarification
    error:          str | None                    = Field(default=None)


class ComplianceOrchestrationResponse(BaseModel):
    """
    Full response returned by the Streamlit chat handler.

    Includes the complete agent stage audit trail so the UI can light up
    the correct stage pills and show the generated SQL + result data.
    """

    question_id:            str | None                    = Field(default=None)
    # The normalised question after IntentChecker processes it
    interpreted_question:   str | None                    = Field(default=None)
    # True when the intent checker decides the question needs clarification
    needs_clarification:    bool                          = Field(default=False)
    clarification_question: str | None                    = Field(default=None)
    sql_query:              str | None                    = Field(default=None)
    df_output:              list[dict[str, object]] | None = Field(default=None)
    result_columns:         list[str]                     = Field(default_factory=list)
    result_summary:         str | None                    = Field(default=None)
    # Stage tags appended by each node, e.g. ["intent_checker:ok", "executor_agent:ok rows=5"]
    stages:                 list[str]                     = Field(default_factory=list)
    error:                  str | None                    = Field(default=None)


class LLMConnectionResponse(BaseModel):
    """Result of the LLM connection test triggered from the config page."""

    success:  bool = Field(description="True when the test request succeeded.")
    provider: str  = Field(description="Configured LLM provider name.")
    message:  str  = Field(description="Human-readable connection status message.")


class HealthResponse(BaseModel):
    """Liveness probe response — always returns status='ok' when the app is up."""

    status:  str = Field(default="ok")
    version: str = Field(default="3.0.0")


# FACTORY HELPERS

def build_chat_response(
    question_id:    str | None,
    sql_query:      str | None,
    df_output:      list[dict[str, Any]] | None,
    result_columns: list[str] | None,
    result_summary: str | None,
    error:          str | None,
) -> ComplianceChatResponse:
    """Assemble a ComplianceChatResponse from individual pipeline output values."""
    return ComplianceChatResponse(
        question_id=question_id,
        sql_query=sql_query,
        df_output=df_output,
        result_columns=result_columns or [],  # never pass None to a list field
        result_summary=result_summary,
        error=error,
    )


def build_orchestration_response(
    question_id:            str | None,
    interpreted_question:   str | None = None,
    needs_clarification:    bool = False,
    clarification_question: str | None = None,
    sql_query:              str | None = None,
    df_output:              list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
    result_columns:         list[str] | None = None,
    result_summary:         str | None = None,
    stages:                 list[str] | None = None,
    error:                  str | None = None,
) -> ComplianceOrchestrationResponse:
    """Assemble the full orchestration response from individual pipeline outputs."""
    return ComplianceOrchestrationResponse(
        question_id=question_id,
        interpreted_question=interpreted_question,
        needs_clarification=needs_clarification,
        clarification_question=clarification_question,
        sql_query=sql_query,
        df_output=df_output,
        result_columns=result_columns or [],
        result_summary=result_summary,
        stages=stages or [],
        error=error,
    )


def build_error_response(
    question_id:   str | None,
    error_message: str,
    stage_tag:     str = "pipeline:error",
) -> ComplianceOrchestrationResponse:
    """Build a minimal error response with only the error field and a stage tag."""
    return ComplianceOrchestrationResponse(
        question_id=question_id,
        error=error_message,
        stages=[stage_tag],  # single tag describes where the failure happened
    )


def build_health_response(version: str = "3.0.0") -> HealthResponse:
    """Return a healthy HealthResponse with status='ok'."""
    return HealthResponse(status="ok", version=version)


def build_llm_connection_response(
    success:  bool,
    provider: str,
    message:  str,
) -> LLMConnectionResponse:
    """Build the result of a connection-test indicating success or failure."""
    return LLMConnectionResponse(success=success, provider=provider, message=message)

"""

These models are the single source of truth for the data contract between

class LLMConnectionResponse(BaseModel):
    """Result of the LLM connection test triggered from the config page."""

    success:  bool = Field(description="True when the test request succeeded.")
    provider: str  = Field(description="Configured LLM provider name.")
    message:  str  = Field(description="Human-readable connection status message.")


class HealthResponse(BaseModel):
    """Liveness probe response — always returns status='ok' when the app is up."""

    status:  str = Field(default="ok")
    version: str = Field(default="3.0.0")


# FACTORY HELPERS

def build_chat_response(
    question_id:    str | None,
    sql_query:      str | None,
    df_output:      list[dict[str, Any]] | None,
    result_columns: list[str] | None,
    result_summary: str | None,
    error:          str | None,
) -> ComplianceChatResponse:
    """Assemble a ComplianceChatResponse from individual pipeline output values."""
    return ComplianceChatResponse(
        question_id=question_id,
        sql_query=sql_query,
        df_output=df_output,
        result_columns=result_columns or [],  # never pass None to a list field
        result_summary=result_summary,
        error=error,
    )


def build_orchestration_response(
    question_id:            str | None,
    interpreted_question:   str | None = None,
    needs_clarification:    bool = False,
    clarification_question: str | None = None,
    sql_query:              str | None = None,
    df_output:              list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
    result_columns:         list[str] | None = None,
    result_summary:         str | None = None,
    stages:                 list[str] | None = None,
    error:                  str | None = None,
) -> ComplianceOrchestrationResponse:
    """Assemble the full orchestration response from individual pipeline outputs."""
    return ComplianceOrchestrationResponse(
        question_id=question_id,
        interpreted_question=interpreted_question,
        needs_clarification=needs_clarification,
        clarification_question=clarification_question,
        sql_query=sql_query,
        df_output=df_output,
        result_columns=result_columns or [],
        result_summary=result_summary,
        stages=stages or [],
        error=error,
    )


def build_error_response(
    question_id:   str | None,
    error_message: str,
    stage_tag:     str = "pipeline:error",
) -> ComplianceOrchestrationResponse:
    """Build a minimal error response with only the error field and a stage tag."""
    return ComplianceOrchestrationResponse(
        question_id=question_id,
        error=error_message,
        stages=[stage_tag],  # single tag describes where the failure happened
    )


def build_health_response(version: str = "3.0.0") -> HealthResponse:
    """Return a healthy HealthResponse with status='ok'."""
    return HealthResponse(status="ok", version=version)


def build_llm_connection_response(
    success:  bool,
    provider: str,
    message:  str,
) -> LLMConnectionResponse:
    """Build the result of a connection-test indicating success or failure."""
    return LLMConnectionResponse(success=success, provider=provider, message=message)


####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\genai\nl2sql_agent\__init__.py
####################################################################################################


"""
genai.nl2sql_agent — six-node LangGraph NL2SQL pipeline.

Nodes:
  intent_checker.py   — IntentChecker   (Node 1, rule-based,           reads intent_checker.yaml)
  schema_checker.py   — SchemaChecker   (Node 2, static Iceberg schema, reads schema_checker.yaml)
  sql_writer_agent.py — SQLWriterAgent  (Node 3, LLM call,             reads sql_writer.yaml)
  sql_reviewer.py     — SQLReviewer     (Node 4, regex+registry checks, reads sql_review.yaml)
  sql_validator.py    — SQLValidator    (Node 4.5, sqlglot AST/hive,   no prompt file)
  executor_agent.py   — ExecutorAgent   (Node 5, Iceberg via PandasEngine, reads query.toml)
  graph.py            — StateGraph factory (build_nl2sql_graph + create_graph)

Target data store: Iceberg table ``dev_feature_store.COMP_TBL`` (Hive on Tez / CML Data).
No local SQLite database is used at runtime.
"""

from genai.nl2sql_agent.graph import build_nl2sql_graph, create_graph
from genai.nl2sql_agent.sql_writer_agent import ContextBuilder
from genai.nl2sql_agent.state import AgentState

__all__ = [
    "AgentState",
    "ContextBuilder",
    "build_nl2sql_graph",
    "create_graph",
]



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\genai\nl2sql_agent\executor_agent.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Executor Agent — Node 5 of the NL2SQL LangGraph pipeline.

Executes the validated SQL SELECT statement against the Iceberg table
``dev_feature_store.COMP_TBL`` via ``PandasEngine`` (Hive on Tez / CML Data
connection), converts the result into a list of column→value dictionaries,
and produces a concise human-readable summary.

This module also contains result formatting helpers:
  - format_summary    — one-sentence result summary
  - to_markdown_table — GitHub-Flavoured Markdown table
  - to_csv            — CSV export with header row
  - extract_scalar    — pull single value from aggregate result

Row-limit policy (controlled via genai/config/query.toml)
---------------------------------------------------------
enforce_default_limit = true
    If the SQL has no LIMIT clause, append "LIMIT <default_limit>" before
    executing so unintentionally large result sets are never returned.

enforce_default_limit = false
    Execute the SQL exactly as written — no automatic LIMIT is added.

max_display_rows
    Hard ceiling on rows returned to the UI regardless of any LIMIT in the SQL.
"""

import csv
import io
import re
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from data._pandas_backend import PandasEngine
from genai.nl2sql_agent.state import AgentState
from utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.executor_agent")

_LIMIT_RE = re.compile(r"\bLIMIT\b", re.IGNORECASE)

# Config TOML path
_QUERY_TOML = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2] / "genai" / "config" / "query.toml"


def _load_query_config() -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Load query.toml — returns defaults on any failure."""
    try:
        try:
            import tomllib
        except ImportError:
            import tomli as tomllib  # type: ignore[no-redef]
        with _QUERY_TOML.open("rb") as fh:
            return tomllib.load(fh).get("query", {})
    except Exception:
        return {}


_Q: dict[str, Any]     = _load_query_config()
_ENFORCE_LIMIT: bool   = bool(_Q.get("enforce_default_limit", True))
_DEFAULT_LIMIT: int    = int(_Q.get("default_limit", 10))
_MAX_DISPLAY_ROWS: int = int(_Q.get("max_display_rows", 500)) or 500


def _ensure_limit(sql: str) -> tuple[str, bool]:
    """
    Apply the row-limit policy from query.toml.

    Returns *(effective_sql, injected)* where *injected* is True when
    ``LIMIT <default_limit>`` was automatically appended.

    When ``enforce_default_limit = false`` this is a no-op.
    """
    if not _ENFORCE_LIMIT:
        return sql, False
    stripped = sql.rstrip("; \t\n")
    if _LIMIT_RE.search(stripped):
        return sql, False          # SQL already has a LIMIT — leave it alone.
    return f"{stripped} LIMIT {_DEFAULT_LIMIT}", True


def format_summary(
    rows: list[dict[str, object]],
    columns: list[str],
    limit_injected: bool = False,
    default_limit: int = _DEFAULT_LIMIT,
    max_display_rows: int = _MAX_DISPLAY_ROWS,
) -> str:
    """
    Build a one-sentence result summary shown to the user.

    Handles three cases:
    - No rows → tells the user nothing matched.
    - Single-cell aggregate (COUNT, SUM …) → highlights the value.
    - Multi-row result → reports count and column names.
    """
    count = len(rows)

    if count == 0:
        return "The query executed successfully but returned no matching records."

    # Single row, single column — probably an aggregate like COUNT(*).
    if count == 1 and len(rows[0]) == 1:
        col, val = next(iter(rows[0].items()))
        return f"Result: **{col}** = `{val}`"

    if count >= max_display_rows:
        limit_note = f" (first {max_display_rows} shown — add a LIMIT clause to narrow results)"
    elif limit_injected:
        limit_note = (
            f" (showing first {default_limit} rows — "
            f"ask for more with 'show top N' or set enforce_default_limit=false in query.toml)"
        )
    else:
        limit_note = ""

    col_list = ", ".join(columns) if columns else ""
    return f"Query returned **{count} record(s)**{limit_note}. Columns: {col_list}."


def to_markdown_table(
    rows: list[dict[str, object]],
    columns: list[str],
    max_rows: int = 20,
) -> str:
    """
    Render rows as a GitHub-Flavoured Markdown table string.

    Includes at most max_rows data rows; appends a note if rows were trimmed.
    Returns "(no rows)" if the result set is empty.
    """
    if not rows:
        return "(no rows)"

    header    = "| " + " | ".join(columns) + " |"
    separator = "| " + " | ".join("---" for _ in columns) + " |"

    data_rows = []
    for row in rows[:max_rows]:
        cells = [str(row.get(col, "")) for col in columns]
        data_rows.append("| " + " | ".join(cells) + " |")

    lines = [header, separator, *data_rows]
    if len(rows) > max_rows:
        lines.append(f"*… {len(rows) - max_rows} more row(s) not shown.*")

    return "\n".join(lines)


def to_csv(rows: list[dict[str, object]], columns: list[str]) -> str:
    """Render rows as a CSV string with a header row."""
    output = io.StringIO()
    writer = csv.DictWriter(
        output, fieldnames=columns, extrasaction="ignore", lineterminator="\n"
    )
    writer.writeheader()
    writer.writerows(rows)
    return output.getvalue()


def extract_scalar(rows: list[dict[str, object]]) -> object | None:
    """
    Pull the single value from a one-row, one-column result (e.g. COUNT(*)).

    Returns None if the result has more than one row or more than one column.
    """
    if len(rows) == 1 and len(rows[0]) == 1:
        return next(iter(rows[0].values()))
    return None


class ExecutorAgent:
    """
    LangGraph node that executes the approved SQL query against the Iceberg
    table ``dev_feature_store.COMP_TBL`` via ``PandasEngine`` and formats
    the results.

    Row-limit behaviour is driven entirely by genai/config/query.toml —
    no code changes needed to adjust limits.

    Parameters
    ----------
    connection_name : str
        CML Data connection name used by ``PandasEngine`` to reach Hive / Iceberg.
        Defaults to the same value set in ``PandasEngine``.
    """

    def __init__(self, connection_name: str = "DLH-DEV-UAT - Hive on Tez") -> None:
        self._engine = PandasEngine(connection_name=connection_name)

    def run(self, state: AgentState) -> AgentState:
        """Execute the SQL query against the Iceberg table and return formatted results."""
        sql: str = state.get("sql_query", "").strip()

        LOGGER.info("ExecutorAgent — executing SQL on Iceberg: %s", sql)

        if not sql:
            msg = "ExecutorAgent received an empty SQL query."
            LOGGER.error(msg)
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                result_rows=[],
                result_columns=[],
                result_summary="No query to execute.",
                error=msg,
                stages=["executor_agent:empty_sql"],
            )

        rows, columns, error_msg, limit_injected = self._execute(sql)

        if error_msg:
            LOGGER.error("ExecutorAgent — SQL execution error: %s", error_msg)
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                result_rows=[],
                result_columns=[],
                result_summary="Query execution failed.",
                error=error_msg,
                stages=["executor_agent:error"],
            )

        summary = format_summary(rows, columns, limit_injected)
        LOGGER.info("ExecutorAgent — %d rows returned.", len(rows))

        return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
            result_rows=rows,
            result_columns=columns,
            result_summary=summary,
            error=None,
            stages=[f"executor_agent:ok rows={len(rows)}"],
        )

    def _execute(
        self,
        sql: str,
    ) -> tuple[list[dict[str, object]], list[str], str | None, bool]:
        """
        Run *sql* via ``PandasEngine.read_table()`` (Iceberg / Hive on Tez).

        Returns ``(rows, column_names, error_message, limit_injected)``.
        """
        effective_sql, limit_injected = _ensure_limit(sql)
        if limit_injected:
            LOGGER.info(
                "ExecutorAgent — no LIMIT in SQL; auto-applied LIMIT %d (query.toml).",
                _DEFAULT_LIMIT,
            )

        try:
            df = self._engine.read_table(effective_sql)
            # Trim to the hard ceiling
            if len(df) > _MAX_DISPLAY_ROWS:
                df = df.iloc[:_MAX_DISPLAY_ROWS]

            col_names: list[str] = list(df.columns)
            rows: list[dict[str, object]] = df.to_dict(orient="records")
            return rows, col_names, None, limit_injected

        except Exception as exc:
            return [], [], f"Iceberg query error: {exc} | Query: {effective_sql}", False



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\genai\nl2sql_agent\graph.py
####################################################################################################



"""
genai/nl2sql_agent/graph.py — LangGraph NL2SQL StateGraph factory.

Wires the six pipeline nodes and exposes:
  - build_nl2sql_graph — full constructor, all parameters explicit
  - create_graph       — convenience wrapper using AppSettings defaults

Pipeline topology:
  [START] → intent_checker
               ├─(intent_ok=False)→ clarify_node → [END]
               └─(intent_ok=True) → schema_checker
                                      └→ sql_writer_agent ←──────────────┐
                                           └→ sql_reviewer                │ retry
                                                ├─(passed)→ sql_validator─┤
                                                ├─(error) → executor      │
                                                └─(fail)  → sql_writer ───┘
                                                    │
                                           sql_validator
                                                ├─(valid)  → executor_agent → [END]
                                                └─(invalid)→ sql_writer_agent (retry)

The graph targets the Iceberg table ``dev_feature_store.COMP_TBL`` on Hive.
No SQLite database path is required — ``SchemaChecker`` and ``ExecutorAgent``
both connect to Iceberg directly.
"""

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Literal

from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from langgraph.graph.state import CompiledStateGraph

from genai.core.model import ModelLoaderService
from genai.nl2sql_agent.executor_agent import ExecutorAgent
from genai.nl2sql_agent.intent_checker import IntentChecker
from genai.nl2sql_agent.schema_checker import SchemaChecker
from genai.nl2sql_agent.sql_reviewer import SQLReviewer
from genai.nl2sql_agent.sql_validator import SQLValidator
from genai.nl2sql_agent.sql_writer_agent import SQLWriterAgent
from genai.nl2sql_agent.state import AgentState
from utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.graph")

NODE_INTENT:    str = "intent_checker"
NODE_SCHEMA:    str = "schema_checker"
NODE_WRITER:    str = "sql_writer_agent"
NODE_REVIEWER:  str = "sql_reviewer"
NODE_VALIDATOR: str = "sql_validator"
NODE_EXECUTOR:  str = "executor_agent"
NODE_CLARIFY:   str = "clarify_node"


# ROUTING FUNCTIONS

def _route_after_intent(state: AgentState) -> Literal["schema_checker", "clarify_node"]:
    """After IntentChecker: clear intent → schema; unclear/blocked → clarify."""
    return NODE_SCHEMA if state.get("intent_ok", False) else NODE_CLARIFY


def _route_after_reviewer(
    state: AgentState,
) -> Literal["sql_validator", "sql_writer_agent", "executor_agent"]:
    """
    After SQLReviewer:
      - Hard error (max retries hit) → executor surfaces the error message.
      - Passed all checks            → hand off to AST validator.
      - Failed but retries left      → send back to SQL writer with feedback.
    """
    if state.get("error"):
        return NODE_EXECUTOR
    if state.get("sql_review_passed", False):
        return NODE_VALIDATOR
    return NODE_WRITER


def _route_after_validator(
    state: AgentState,
) -> Literal["executor_agent", "sql_writer_agent"]:
    """After SQLValidator: valid AST → executor; invalid → retry the writer."""
    return NODE_EXECUTOR if state.get("sqlglot_valid", False) else NODE_WRITER


def _clarify_node(state: AgentState) -> AgentState:
    """
    Terminal node reached when intent is too ambiguous to process.

    Appends a stage tag and returns — the graph then routes to END.
    """
    LOGGER.info("ClarifyNode — ending graph (clarification requested).")
    return AgentState(stages=["clarify_node:terminal"])  # type: ignore[return-value]


# GRAPH FACTORY

def build_nl2sql_graph(
    model_service:      ModelLoaderService,
    iceberg_table:      str = "dev_feature_store.COMP_TBL",
    connection_name:    str = "DLH-DEV-UAT - Hive on Tez",
    max_review_retries: int = 3,
    sqlglot_dialect:    str = "hive",
) -> CompiledStateGraph:  # type: ignore[name-defined]
    """
    Construct and compile the six-node NL2SQL StateGraph.

    All nodes run queries against the Iceberg table on Hive — no local SQLite
    file is required.

    Args:
        model_service      — loaded ModelLoaderService for LLM inference
        iceberg_table      — fully-qualified Iceberg table (default: dev_feature_store.COMP_TBL)
        connection_name    — CML Data connection name for PandasEngine / ExecutorAgent
        max_review_retries — maximum writer → reviewer retry cycles (default 3)
        sqlglot_dialect    — sqlglot dialect for AST parsing (default "hive")

    Returns:
        A compiled LangGraph StateGraph ready to call .invoke() or .stream() on.
    """
    LOGGER.info(
        "Building NL2SQL LangGraph — table=%s retries=%d dialect=%s",
        iceberg_table, max_review_retries, sqlglot_dialect,
    )

    # ── Instantiate all six node objects ──────────────────────────────────────
    intent_checker = IntentChecker()
    schema_checker = SchemaChecker(iceberg_table=iceberg_table)
    sql_writer     = SQLWriterAgent(model_service=model_service)
    sql_reviewer   = SQLReviewer(max_retries=max_review_retries)
    sql_validator  = SQLValidator(dialect=sqlglot_dialect)
    executor       = ExecutorAgent(connection_name=connection_name)

    # ── Build the StateGraph with AgentState as the shared state type ─────────
    graph = StateGraph(AgentState)

    # Register each node — the callable is the node's .run() method
    graph.add_node(NODE_INTENT,    intent_checker.run)
    graph.add_node(NODE_SCHEMA,    schema_checker.run)
    graph.add_node(NODE_WRITER,    sql_writer.run)
    graph.add_node(NODE_REVIEWER,  sql_reviewer.run)
    graph.add_node(NODE_VALIDATOR, sql_validator.run)
    graph.add_node(NODE_EXECUTOR,  executor.run)
    graph.add_node(NODE_CLARIFY,   _clarify_node)

    # ── Wire edges ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    graph.add_edge(START, NODE_INTENT)

    graph.add_conditional_edges(
        NODE_INTENT,
        _route_after_intent,
        {NODE_SCHEMA: NODE_SCHEMA, NODE_CLARIFY: NODE_CLARIFY},
    )

    graph.add_edge(NODE_SCHEMA,  NODE_WRITER)
    graph.add_edge(NODE_WRITER,  NODE_REVIEWER)

    graph.add_conditional_edges(
        NODE_REVIEWER,
        _route_after_reviewer,
        {NODE_VALIDATOR: NODE_VALIDATOR, NODE_EXECUTOR: NODE_EXECUTOR, NODE_WRITER: NODE_WRITER},
    )

    graph.add_conditional_edges(
        NODE_VALIDATOR,
        _route_after_validator,
        {NODE_EXECUTOR: NODE_EXECUTOR, NODE_WRITER: NODE_WRITER},
    )

    graph.add_edge(NODE_EXECUTOR, END)
    graph.add_edge(NODE_CLARIFY,  END)

    # ── Compile ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    compiled = graph.compile()
    LOGGER.info("NL2SQL LangGraph compiled successfully.")
    return compiled


def create_graph(
    model_service:      ModelLoaderService,
    iceberg_table:      str | None = None,
    connection_name:    str | None = None,
    max_review_retries: int = 3,
    sqlglot_dialect:    str = "hive",
) -> CompiledStateGraph:  # type: ignore[name-defined]
    """
    Convenience wrapper — iceberg_table and connection_name default to the
    values configured in AppSettings / PandasEngine.

    Use this when you don't need to override the Iceberg table explicitly.
    """
    from utils.config import get_settings
    settings = get_settings()

    resolved_table: str = iceberg_table or settings.database.iceberg_table
    resolved_conn:  str = connection_name or settings.database.connection_name

    LOGGER.info("create_graph — table=%s retries=%d", resolved_table, max_review_retries)
    return build_nl2sql_graph(
        model_service=model_service,
        iceberg_table=resolved_table,
        connection_name=resolved_conn,
        max_review_retries=max_review_retries,
        sqlglot_dialect=sqlglot_dialect,
    )



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\genai\nl2sql_agent\intent_checker.py
####################################################################################################








"""
genai/nl2sql_agent/intent_checker.py — Node 1 of the NL2SQL pipeline.

Classifies whether the user question has clear enough intent to proceed.
All user-facing messages are loaded from genai/prompt/intent_checker.yaml —
no message strings are hardcoded in this file.

Four checks (rule-based, no LLM):
  1. Empty question
  2. Disallowed action words (DELETE, DROP, INSERT …)
  3. Too short / meaningless input
  4. Vague / ambiguous phrasing
"""

import re
from typing import Final

from genai.nl2sql_agent.state import AgentState
from utils.logger import get_logger
from utils.registry import get_prompt

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.intent_checker")

_YAML = "intent_checker.yaml"

_METRIC_TERMS: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    {
        "age", "kyc", "balance", "maturity", "product", "branch", "circle",
        "senior", "account", "rate", "deposit", "customer", "flag", "date",
        "interest", "tenor", "brcd", "count", "sum", "total", "list", "show",
        "top", "max", "min", "average",
    }
)

_VAGUE_PHRASES: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    {
        "tell me something", "give me data", "get data", "details", "info",
        "information", "data", "give me", "tell me",
    }
)

_AMBIGUOUS_TOKENS: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    {"this", "that", "those", "these", "it", "something"}
)

_MIN_MEANINGFUL_TOKENS: Final[int] = 3
_MIN_TOKEN_LEN: Final[int] = 2
_RICH_QUESTION_THRESHOLD: Final[int] = 3

_STOP_WORDS: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    {
        "a", "an", "the", "is", "are", "was", "were", "be", "been", "being",
        "have", "has", "had", "do", "does", "did", "will", "would", "could",
        "should", "may", "might", "shall", "can", "need", "dare", "ought",
        "used", "to", "of", "in", "on", "at", "by", "for", "with", "about",
        "against", "between", "through", "during", "before", "after", "above",
        "below", "from", "up", "down", "into", "out", "off", "over", "under",
        "again", "then", "once", "and", "but", "or", "nor", "so", "yet",
        "both", "either", "neither", "not", "no", "only", "own", "same",
        "than", "too", "very", "just", "because", "as", "until", "while",
        "i", "me", "my", "we", "our", "you", "your", "he", "she", "they",
        "him", "her", "his", "its", "their", "what", "which", "who", "whom",
        "how", "when", "where", "why", "all", "any", "each", "every", "some",
        "few", "more", "most", "other", "such", "get", "give", "tell", "let",
        "please", "hi", "hello", "hey", "ok", "okay", "thanks", "thank",
    }
)

_PUNCT_STRIP: re.Pattern[str] = re.compile(r"[^\w]")

_DISALLOWED_ACTION_WORDS: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    {
        "delete", "drop", "insert", "update", "truncate", "alter", "create",
        "replace", "pragma", "attach", "detach", "vacuum", "modify", "remove",
        "erase", "wipe", "purge", "destroy",
    }
)


class IntentChecker:
    """
    LangGraph node that classifies user intent.

    All messages sourced from intent_checker.yaml — edit that file to change
    the wording shown to users without touching Python code.
    """

    def run(self, state: AgentState) -> AgentState:
        """Classify intent through four sequential rule-based checks."""
        question: str = state.get("question", "").strip()
        LOGGER.info("IntentChecker — analysing: %r", question)

        # ── Check 1: empty question ────────────────────────────────────────
        if not question:
            LOGGER.warning("IntentChecker — empty question.")
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                interpreted_question="",
                intent_ok=False,
                clarification_needed=get_prompt(
                    _YAML, "messages", "empty_question",
                    default=(
                        "No question was provided. Please ask a compliance query, "
                        "for example: \"How many accounts have KYC flag N?\""
                    ),
                ),
                stages=["intent_checker:empty"],
            )

        lower_q     = question.lower()
        raw_tokens  = lower_q.split()
        clean_tokens = {_PUNCT_STRIP.sub("", t) for t in raw_tokens if _PUNCT_STRIP.sub("", t)}

        # ── Check 2: disallowed action words ──────────────────────────────
        matched = _DISALLOWED_ACTION_WORDS & clean_tokens
        if matched:
            blocked_word = min(matched).upper()
            LOGGER.warning("IntentChecker — disallowed action word: %s", blocked_word)
            template = get_prompt(_YAML, "messages", "disallowed_action", default="")
            msg = template.format(blocked_word=blocked_word) if template else (
                f"Sorry, the keyword <strong>{blocked_word}</strong> is not permitted. "
                "This system is <strong>read-only</strong> — only SELECT queries are supported."
            )
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                interpreted_question=self._normalise(question),
                intent_ok=False,
                clarification_needed=msg,
                stages=[f"intent_checker:disallowed_action_word={blocked_word}"],
            )

        # ── Check 3: too short / meaningless ──────────────────────────────
        has_metric = bool(_METRIC_TERMS & clean_tokens) or any(m in lower_q for m in _METRIC_TERMS)
        meaningful = {
            t for t in clean_tokens
            if t not in _STOP_WORDS and len(t) >= _MIN_TOKEN_LEN and not t.isdigit()
        }
        if len(meaningful) < _MIN_MEANINGFUL_TOKENS and not has_metric:
            LOGGER.info("IntentChecker — too short (%d tokens): %r", len(meaningful), question)
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                interpreted_question=self._normalise(question),
                intent_ok=False,
                clarification_needed=get_prompt(
                    _YAML, "messages", "too_short",
                    default="Your question is too short or unclear to process.",
                ),
                stages=["intent_checker:too_short"],
            )

        # ── Check 4: vague / ambiguous phrasing ───────────────────────────
        is_pure_vague   = lower_q.strip("?. ") in _VAGUE_PHRASES
        has_ambiguous   = bool(_AMBIGUOUS_TOKENS & clean_tokens)
        rich_question   = len(meaningful) > _RICH_QUESTION_THRESHOLD
        needs_clarification = is_pure_vague or (has_ambiguous and not has_metric and not rich_question)

        interpreted = self._normalise(question)

        if needs_clarification:
            LOGGER.info("IntentChecker — clarification required: %r", question)
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                interpreted_question=interpreted,
                intent_ok=False,
                clarification_needed=get_prompt(
                    _YAML, "messages", "clarification",
                    default="Your question is unclear. Please rephrase with more detail.",
                ),
                stages=["intent_checker:clarification_required"],
            )

        LOGGER.info("IntentChecker — intent OK: %r", interpreted)
        return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
            interpreted_question=interpreted,
            intent_ok=True,
            clarification_needed=None,
            stages=["intent_checker:ok"],
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _normalise(question: str) -> str:
        """Strip whitespace and ensure a trailing question mark."""
        q = question.strip()
        if q and not q.endswith("?"):
            q = q.rstrip(".") + "?"
        return q



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\genai\nl2sql_agent\schema_checker.py
####################################################################################################

"""
genai/nl2sql_agent/schema_checker.py — Node 2 of the NL2SQL pipeline.

Builds a Markdown-formatted schema context string for the SQL Writer to
embed in its LLM prompt.  The authoritative schema comes from two sources:

  1. ``genai/config/databases.toml`` — table descriptions, column descriptions,
     and few-shot examples (loaded via the registry).
  2. ``data/iceberg_schema.txt`` — Iceberg DDL types used to annotate each
     column with its exact Hive/Iceberg data type.

No live database connection is made here — all introspection has been
replaced with static metadata from the configuration files above.  This is
correct because the target store is an Iceberg table on Hive (not a local
SQLite file) and schema reads against the live cluster are unnecessary.

Configuration (sample_row_limit, header templates) is still loaded from
genai/prompt/schema_checker.yaml.
"""

import re
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from genai.nl2sql_agent.state import AgentState
from utils.logger import get_logger
from utils.registry import get_prompt, get_table_meta, load_prompt

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.schema_checker")

_YAML     = "schema_checker.yaml"
_CFG      = load_prompt(_YAML)

# Path to the Iceberg DDL file — used to extract Hive column types
_ICEBERG_SCHEMA_FILE = (
    Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3] / "data" / "iceberg_schema.txt"
)

# Iceberg table identifier — the single table this pipeline queries
_ICEBERG_TABLE = "dev_feature_store.COMP_TBL"
_TABLE_NAME    = "COMP_TBL"

# Pre-compiled pattern to extract  <col_name>  <TYPE>  from the DDL
_COL_TYPE_RE = re.compile(
    r"^\s{4}(\w+)\s+(STRING|DATE|INT|DECIMAL\(\d+,\s*\d+\))",
    re.IGNORECASE | re.MULTILINE,
)


def _load_iceberg_types() -> dict[str, str]:
    """
    Parse ``data/iceberg_schema.txt`` and return {COLUMN_NAME: HIVE_TYPE}.

    Returns an empty dict if the file is missing or unparseable — the schema
    context will still be built from databases.toml alone.
    """
    if not _ICEBERG_SCHEMA_FILE.exists():
        LOGGER.warning(
            "SchemaChecker — iceberg_schema.txt not found at %s; skipping type annotations.",
            _ICEBERG_SCHEMA_FILE,
        )
        return {}
    try:
        text = _ICEBERG_SCHEMA_FILE.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        return {m.group(1).upper(): m.group(2).upper() for m in _COL_TYPE_RE.finditer(text)}
    except Exception:
        LOGGER.exception("SchemaChecker — failed to parse iceberg_schema.txt")
        return {}


# Module-level cache — parse once on first import
_ICEBERG_TYPES: dict[str, str] = _load_iceberg_types()


class SchemaChecker:
    """
    LangGraph node — builds the Markdown schema context for the SQL Writer.

    The schema is constructed entirely from static config files:
      - ``databases.toml``      → column business descriptions + few-shots
      - ``iceberg_schema.txt``  → Hive/Iceberg column data types

    Parameters
    ----------
    iceberg_table : str
        Fully qualified Iceberg table name written into ``db_path`` on the
        state so downstream nodes (reviewer, etc.) know the target table.
        Defaults to ``dev_feature_store.COMP_TBL``.
    """

    def __init__(self, iceberg_table: str = _ICEBERG_TABLE) -> None:
        self._iceberg_table = iceberg_table

    def run(self, state: AgentState) -> AgentState:
        """Build the Iceberg schema context string and inject it into state."""
        question: str = state.get("interpreted_question", state.get("question", ""))
        LOGGER.info("SchemaChecker — building Iceberg schema context for: %r", question)

        context = self._build_context()

        LOGGER.info("SchemaChecker — using Iceberg table=%s", self._iceberg_table)
        return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
            db_path=self._iceberg_table,          # downstream nodes read this as the target table
            schema_context=context,
            stages=[f"schema_checker:table={self._iceberg_table}"],
        )

    def _build_context(self) -> str:
        """Build the Markdown schema context block for LLM injection."""
        schema_header   = get_prompt(_YAML, "messages", "schema_header",   default="## Database: `{db_stem}`")
        table_header    = get_prompt(_YAML, "messages", "table_header",    default="### Table: `{table_name}`")
        col_desc_header = get_prompt(
            _YAML, "messages", "col_desc_header",
            default="**Column descriptions for `{table_name}` (from databases.toml):**",
        )

        lines: list[str] = [
            schema_header.format(db_stem="dev_feature_store"),  # type: ignore[arg-type]
            "",
            f"> Target Iceberg table: `{self._iceberg_table}`",
            "",
        ]

        # ── Schema table: column name + Hive type ────────────────────────────
        lines.append(table_header.format(table_name=_TABLE_NAME))  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        lines.append("")
        lines.append("| # | Column | Hive/Iceberg Type |")
        lines.append("|---|--------|-------------------|")

        toml_meta: dict[str, Any] = get_table_meta(_TABLE_NAME)
        toml_cols: dict[str, str] = toml_meta.get("columns", {})

        for idx, col_name in enumerate(toml_cols):
            col_type = _ICEBERG_TYPES.get(col_name.upper(), "STRING")
            lines.append(f"| {idx} | `{col_name}` | {col_type} |")

        lines.append("")

        # ── Business descriptions from databases.toml ─────────────────────────
        if toml_cols:
            lines.append(col_desc_header.format(table_name=_TABLE_NAME))  # type: ignore[arg-type]
            lines.append("")
            for col_name, col_desc in toml_cols.items():
                lines.append(f"- `{col_name}`: {col_desc}")
            lines.append("")

        # ── Table-level description ───────────────────────────────────────────
        tbl_desc: str = toml_meta.get("description", "")
        if tbl_desc:
            lines.append(f"**Table description:** {tbl_desc}")
            lines.append("")

        return "\n".join(lines)



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\genai\nl2sql_agent\sql_reviewer.py
####################################################################################################








"""
genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_reviewer.py — Node 4 of the NL2SQL pipeline.

Validates the SQL statement produced by the SQL Writer before it reaches
the SQL Validator (Node 4.5) and the Executor.

ALL user-facing and reviewer feedback strings are loaded from
genai/prompt/sql_review.yaml — no message strings are hardcoded here.

Also contains RetryHandler (retry count management).

Four validation checks:
  1. Safety    — retry ceiling guard
  2. Safety    — disallowed DML/DDL keywords
  3. Structure — must start with SELECT and contain FROM
  4. Schema    — every referenced table must exist in the live database
"""

import re
from typing import Final

from genai.nl2sql_agent.state import AgentState
from utils.logger import get_logger
from utils.registry import get_all_tables, get_prompt

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.sql_reviewer")

_YAML = "sql_review.yaml"

_DISALLOWED_KEYWORDS: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    {
        "INSERT", "UPDATE", "DELETE", "DROP", "TRUNCATE", "ALTER", "CREATE",
        "REPLACE", "PRAGMA", "ATTACH", "DETACH", "VACUUM",
    }
)

_FROM_PATTERN:  Final[re.Pattern[str]] = re.compile(r"\bFROM\b",  re.IGNORECASE)
_TABLE_PATTERN: Final[re.Pattern[str]] = re.compile(
    r"\bFROM\s+([`\"\[]?[\w.]+[`\"\]]?)"
    r"|\bJOIN\s+([`\"\[]?[\w.]+[`\"\]]?)",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)
_MAX_ALLOWED_RETRIES: Final[int] = 3

# Known Iceberg tables — sourced from databases.toml at import time.
# The reviewer uses this set instead of live SQLite PRAGMA queries.
_KNOWN_TABLES: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    t.upper() for t in get_all_tables()
)



class RetryHandler:
    """Keeps retry count and enforces the ceiling."""

    def __init__(self, max_retries: int = _MAX_ALLOWED_RETRIES) -> None:
        self._max = max_retries

    @property
    def max_retries(self) -> int:
        return self._max

    def current_count(self, state: AgentState) -> int:
        return int(state.get("retry_count", 0))

    def should_retry(self, state: AgentState) -> bool:
        return self.current_count(state) < self._max

    def increment(self, state: AgentState) -> AgentState:
        n = self.current_count(state) + 1
        LOGGER.info("RetryHandler — retry_count → %d (max=%d)", n, self._max)
        return AgentState(retry_count=n)  # type: ignore[return-value]



class SQLReviewer:
    """
    LangGraph node — safety and schema validation on generated SQL.

    All feedback/error messages come from sql_review.yaml.
    max_retries — maximum writer → reviewer cycles before giving up.
    """

    def __init__(self, max_retries: int = _MAX_ALLOWED_RETRIES) -> None:
        self._max_retries = max_retries

    def run(self, state: AgentState) -> AgentState:
        """Validate the generated SQL through four sequential checks."""
        sql:   str = state.get("sql_query", "").strip()
        retry: int = state.get("retry_count", 0)

        LOGGER.info("SQLReviewer — reviewing SQL (retry=%d): %s", retry, sql)

        # ── Guard: retry ceiling ───────────────────────────────────────────
        if retry >= self._max_retries:
            msg = get_prompt(
                _YAML, "feedback", "max_retries",
                default=(
                    f"⚠ The request could not be completed after {self._max_retries} attempts. "
                    "Please rephrase your question and try again."
                ),
            )
            msg = msg.format(max_retries=self._max_retries)  # type: ignore[union-attr]
            LOGGER.error("SQLReviewer — max retries exceeded.")
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                sql_review_passed=False,
                review_feedback=msg,
                retry_count=retry,
                stages=["sql_reviewer:max_retries_exceeded"],
                error=msg,
            )

        # ── Guard: empty SQL ───────────────────────────────────────────────
        if not sql:
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                sql_review_passed=False,
                review_feedback=get_prompt(
                    _YAML, "feedback", "empty_sql",
                    default="The SQL Writer returned an empty query.",
                ),
                retry_count=retry + 1,
                stages=["sql_reviewer:empty_sql"],
            )

        # ── Check 1: disallowed keywords ──────────────────────────────────
        upper_sql = sql.upper()
        for kw in _DISALLOWED_KEYWORDS:
            if re.search(rf"\b{kw}\b", upper_sql):
                LOGGER.warning("SQLReviewer — disallowed keyword: %s", kw)
                feedback_tpl = get_prompt(_YAML, "feedback", "disallowed_kw", default="")
                error_tpl    = get_prompt(_YAML, "user_error", "disallowed_kw", default="")
                return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                    sql_review_passed=False,
                    review_feedback=(feedback_tpl or "").format(kw=kw),
                    retry_count=retry + 1,
                    stages=[f"sql_reviewer:disallowed_keyword={kw}"],
                    error=(error_tpl or "").format(kw=kw),
                )

        # ── Check 2: must start with SELECT ───────────────────────────────
        if not upper_sql.strip().startswith("SELECT"):
            LOGGER.warning("SQLReviewer — no SELECT prefix.")
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                sql_review_passed=False,
                review_feedback=get_prompt(
                    _YAML, "feedback", "no_select",
                    default="The query must begin with SELECT.",
                ),
                retry_count=retry + 1,
                stages=["sql_reviewer:no_select"],
            )

        # ── Check 3: must contain FROM ────────────────────────────────────
        if not _FROM_PATTERN.search(sql):
            LOGGER.warning("SQLReviewer — missing FROM clause.")
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                sql_review_passed=False,
                review_feedback=get_prompt(
                    _YAML, "feedback", "missing_from",
                    default="The query is missing a FROM clause.",
                ),
                retry_count=retry + 1,
                stages=["sql_reviewer:missing_from"],
            )

        # ── Check 4: all referenced tables must exist in databases.toml ──────
        invalid = self._check_tables(sql)
        if invalid:
            LOGGER.warning("SQLReviewer — unknown tables: %s", invalid)
            tpl = get_prompt(_YAML, "feedback", "unknown_tables", default="")
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                sql_review_passed=False,
                review_feedback=(tpl or "").format(tables=", ".join(invalid)),
                retry_count=retry + 1,
                stages=[f"sql_reviewer:unknown_tables={invalid}"],
            )

        LOGGER.info("SQLReviewer — SQL passed all checks.")
        return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
            sql_review_passed=True,
            review_feedback=None,
            retry_count=retry,
            stages=["sql_reviewer:passed"],
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _check_tables(sql: str) -> list[str]:
        """
        Return table names referenced in *sql* that are not registered in
        databases.toml.

        Fully-qualified Iceberg names (``db.table``) are checked against both
        their bare table name and the full qualified form so that queries like
        ``SELECT … FROM dev_feature_store.COMP_TBL`` pass validation.
        """
        referenced: set[str] = set()
        for m in _TABLE_PATTERN.finditer(sql):
            name = (m.group(1) or m.group(2) or "").strip("`\"[]")
            if name:
                # Accept both qualified (db.table) and bare (table) forms
                bare = name.upper().split(".")[-1]
                referenced.add(bare)
        if not referenced:
            return []
        return sorted(referenced - _KNOWN_TABLES)



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\genai\nl2sql_agent\sql_validator.py
####################################################################################################








"""
genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_validator.py — Node 4.5 of the NL2SQL pipeline.

Uses sqlglot to parse the generated SQL at the AST level before it
reaches the database.  Catches syntax errors and non-SELECT statements
that slipped past the regex-based SQL Reviewer.

Three checks:
  1. Parse validity  — sqlglot must build an AST without errors.
  2. Statement type  — only SELECT statements are allowed.
  3. Dialect round-trip — canonicalise to SQLite syntax.

No prompt file needed — all output is structural pass/fail, no user messages.
"""


from genai.nl2sql_agent.state import AgentState
from utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.sql_validator")

_ALLOWED_STMT_TYPES: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"select"})


class SQLValidator:
    """
    LangGraph node — AST-level SQL validation via sqlglot.

    dialect     — sqlglot dialect for parsing (default: "sqlite")
    strict_mode — reserved for future use
    """

    def __init__(self, dialect: str = "hive", strict_mode: bool = False) -> None:
        self._dialect     = dialect
        self._strict_mode = strict_mode

    def run(self, state: AgentState) -> AgentState:
        """Parse the SQL in state['sql_query'] and decide pass or retry."""
        sql:   str = state.get("sql_query", "").strip()
        retry: int = state.get("retry_count", 0)

        LOGGER.info("SQLValidator — validating: %s", sql)

        # If sqlglot isn't installed, skip and let the executor handle it.
        try:
            import sqlglot
            import sqlglot.errors
        except ImportError:
            LOGGER.warning("SQLValidator — sqlglot not installed; skipping AST check.")
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                sqlglot_valid=True,
                sqlglot_error=None,
                stages=["sql_validator:skipped_no_sqlglot"],
            )

        # Check 1: parse.
        parse_error: str | None = None
        parsed = []
        try:
            parsed = sqlglot.parse(
                sql, dialect=self._dialect,
                error_level=sqlglot.errors.ErrorLevel.RAISE,
            )
        except sqlglot.errors.ParseError as exc:
            parse_error = str(exc)
            LOGGER.warning("SQLValidator — ParseError: %s", parse_error)

        if parse_error:
            return self._fail(
                retry, stage_tag="parse_error",
                reason=f"sqlglot could not parse the SQL: {parse_error}. Use standard Hive/Iceberg SQL syntax.",
            )

        if not parsed or parsed[0] is None:
            return self._fail(
                retry, stage_tag="empty_ast",
                reason="sqlglot returned an empty parse tree. Ensure the query is a valid SELECT.",
            )

        stmt = parsed[0]

        # Check 2: only SELECT allowed.
        stmt_type = type(stmt).__name__.lower()
        if stmt_type not in _ALLOWED_STMT_TYPES:
            LOGGER.warning("SQLValidator — non-SELECT type: %s", stmt_type)
            return self._fail(
                retry, stage_tag=f"disallowed_stmt_type={stmt_type}",
                reason=f"sqlglot identified this as '{stmt_type.upper()}'. Only SELECT is permitted.",
            )

        # Check 3: canonicalise to SQLite dialect.
        try:
            canonical: str = stmt.sql(dialect=self._dialect, pretty=False)
        except Exception:
            canonical = sql

        if canonical and canonical.strip() != sql.strip():
            LOGGER.info("SQLValidator — SQL normalised.\n  Before: %s\n  After : %s", sql, canonical)

        LOGGER.info("SQLValidator — passed AST validation.")
        return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
            sqlglot_valid=True,
            sqlglot_error=None,
            sql_query=canonical or sql,
            stages=[f"sql_validator:passed dialect={self._dialect}"],
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _fail(retry: int, reason: str, stage_tag: str) -> AgentState:
        """Return a partial AgentState marking validation as failed."""
        return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
            sqlglot_valid=False,
            sqlglot_error=reason,
            sql_review_passed=False,
            review_feedback=reason,
            retry_count=retry + 1,
            stages=[f"sql_validator:{stage_tag}"],
        )



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\genai\nl2sql_agent\sql_writer_agent.py
####################################################################################################








"""
genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_writer_agent.py — Node 3 of the NL2SQL pipeline.

Translates the normalised user question into a valid Hive/Iceberg SELECT
statement by calling the configured LLM provider.
