####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\utils\_cml_api.py
####################################################################################################


import logging
from datetime import datetime
from zoneinfo import ZoneInfo

import cmlapi
from cmlapi import CMLServiceApi, CreateJobRequest, models

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

RUNTIME_IDENTIFIER = [
    "hadoop-cli-7.3.1.200-90",
    # This equals to spark 3.5.1; to change spark version probably create dummy job with
    # respective spark version & then use CML Job api to fetch details
    "spark351-7218800-1240-b711",
]
DEFAULT_TIMEOUT = 60 * 10


class CAIClient:
    """Client for interacting with Cloudera AI (CAI) API.

    Parameters
    ----------
    project_id : str
        The CML project identifier.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> client = CAIClient(project_id="my-project-123")

    >>> job_config = client.job_config_template(
    ...     job_prefix_name="data-processing",
    ...     batch_no=1,
    ...     job_script="src/jobs/process_data.py"
    ... )
    >>> job = client.create_job(job_config)
    >>> job_run = client.create_job_run(job.id)
    """

    project_id: str

    @property
    def client(self) -> CMLServiceApi:
        """Get the CML service API client.

        Returns
        -------
        CMLServiceApi
            The default CML API client instance.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> cai = CAIClient()
        >>> api_client = cai.client
        """
        _ = cmlapi.default_client()
        return _

    def job_config_template(
        self,
        job_prefix_name: str,
        batch_no: int | None,
        job_script: str,
        cpu: int = 2,
        memory: int = 8,
        env: dict | None = None,
        runtime_identifier: str = "nexus.dlhdevuat.sbi.bank.in:9443/clouderadataservices/cloudera/cdsw/ml-runtime-jupyterlab-python3.11-ml:2026.03.03.v1",
    ) -> CreateJobRequest:
        """Create a CML job configuration template.

        Generates a job configuration with specified resources and runtime settings.
        If batch_no is provided, it will be included in the job name along with
        the current timestamp.

        Parameters
        ----------
        job_prefix_name : str
            Prefix for the job name.
        batch_no : int or None
            Batch number to include in job name. If None, only prefix is used.
        job_script : str
            Path to the script file to execute (e.g., "src/jobs/process_data.py").
        cpu : int, optional
            Number of CPU cores to allocate, by default 2.
        memory : int, optional
            Memory in GB to allocate, by default 8.
        env : dict or None, optional
            Environment variables as key-value pairs, by default None.
        runtime_identifier : str, optional
            Docker image identifier for the runtime environment.

        Returns
        -------
        CreateJobRequest
            Configured job request object ready for submission.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = CAIClient(project_id="proj-123")
        >>> # Create job config without batch number
        >>> config = client.job_config_template(
        ...     job_prefix_name="etl-pipeline",
        ...     batch_no=None,
        ...     job_script="src/jobs/etl.py",
        ...     cpu=4,
        ...     memory=16
        ... )
        >>>
        >>> # Create job config with batch number
        >>> config = client.job_config_template(
        ...     job_prefix_name="data-processing",
        ...     batch_no=5,
        ...     job_script="src/jobs/process.py",
        ...     env={"ENV": "production", "LOG_LEVEL": "INFO"}
        ... )

        Notes
        -----
        - The job timeout is set to 10 minutes (600 seconds).
        - Jobs are configured to be killed on timeout.
        - Spark runtime addons are automatically included for Spark 3.5.1.
        """
        # If there are batches then only we'll have batch name in job name
        name = (
            f"{job_prefix_name}-batch-{batch_no}-{datetime.now(tz=ZoneInfo('Asia/Kolkata'))!s}"
            if batch_no
            else job_prefix_name
        )

        return cmlapi.CreateJobRequest(
            name=name,
            project_id=self.project_id,
            script=job_script,
            cpu=cpu,
            memory=memory,
            environment=env,
            # NOTE - runtime should match with intended runtime docker image
            runtime_identifier=runtime_identifier,
            runtime_addon_identifiers=RUNTIME_IDENTIFIER,
            timeout=DEFAULT_TIMEOUT,
            kill_on_timeout=True,
        )

    def create_job(self, job_config: CreateJobRequest) -> models.job.Job:
        """Create a new CML job.

        Submits a job configuration to CML and creates the job in the project.

        Parameters
        ----------
        job_config : CreateJobRequest
            Job configuration object created by job_config_template().

        Returns
        -------
        models.job.Job
            The created job object with job ID and metadata.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = CAIClient(project_id="proj-123")

        >>> config = client.job_config_template(
        ...     job_prefix_name="ml-training",
        ...     batch_no=1,
        ...     job_script="src/jobs/train_model.py"
        ... )
        >>> job = client.create_job(config)
        >>> print(f"Job created with ID: {job.id}")
        Job created with ID: job-abc-123

        See Also
        --------
        job_config_template : Create job configuration
        create_job_run : Execute a created job
        """
        job = self.client.create_job(job_config, project_id=self.project_id)
        logger.info(f"successfully created job with {job.id}")
        return job

    def create_job_run(self, job_id: str) -> models.job_run.JobRun:
        """Execute a CML job by creating a job run.

        Creates and starts a new run for an existing job.

        Parameters
        ----------
        job_id : str
            The unique identifier of the job to run.

        Returns
        -------
        models.job_run.JobRun
            The created job run object with run ID and status.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = CAIClient(project_id="proj-123")
        >>> # First create a job
        >>> config = client.job_config_template(
        ...     job_prefix_name="data-pipeline",
        ...     batch_no=None,
        ...     job_script="src/jobs/pipeline.py"
        ... )
        >>> job = client.create_job(config)
        >>>
        >>> # Then execute the job
        >>> job_run = client.create_job_run(job.id)
        >>> print(f"Job run started with ID: {job_run.id}")
        Job run started with ID: run-xyz-456

        See Also
        --------
        create_job : Create a new job
        _job_run_template : Generate job run configuration
        """
        job_run_config = self._job_run_template(job_id=job_id)
        run = self.client.create_job_run(
            job_run_config, job_id=job_id, project_id=self.project_id
        )
        logger.info(f"successfully created job run {run.id}")
        return run

    def _job_run_template(self, job_id: str):
        """Create a job run configuration template.

        Internal method to generate the configuration for executing a job.

        Parameters
        ----------
        job_id : str
            The unique identifier of the job to run.

        Returns
        -------
        cmlapi.CreateJobRunRequest
            Job run request configuration object.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = CAIClient(project_id="proj-123")
        >>> run_config = client._job_run_template(job_id="job-abc-123")
        """
        return cmlapi.CreateJobRunRequest(project_id=self.project_id, job_id=job_id)



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\utils\_hdfs_client.py
####################################################################################################


import logging
import os
import subprocess
import tempfile
from dataclasses import dataclass

import pyarrow.dataset as ds
from pyarrow import fs

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


@dataclass
class HDFSClient:
    """HDFS client for interacting with Hadoop Distributed File System.

    This client provides methods for file operations, data reading, and model loading
    from HDFS using PyArrow filesystem interface and HDFS CLI commands.

    Parameters
    ----------

    host : str, default="default"
        HDFS host connection string. Use "default" to connect to the default
        filesystem configured in Hadoop settings.


    Examples
    --------

    >>> # Initialize HDFS client with default host
    >>> client = HDFSClient()

    >>> # Initialize with specific host (rarely needed)
    >>> client = HDFSClient(host="hdfs://namenode:8020")

    >>> # List files in a directory
    >>> files = client.list_files("/user/some_folder/data")

    >>> # Upload a file to HDFS
    >>> client.upload("local_file.csv", "/user/data/remote_file.csv")

    >>> # Download a file from HDFS
    >>> client.download("/user/data/remote_file.csv", "local_file.csv")

    >>> # Read parquet data
    >>> dataset = client.read_parquet_table("/user/data/parquet_files")
    """

    host: str = "default"

    def __post_init__(self):
        # configuring env variable
        logger.debug("setting env variable for hdfs client")
        os.environ["CLASSPATH"] = os.popen("hdfs classpath").read().strip()
        # TODO - Below line is for cloudera cluster & needs to be handle in better way
        os.environ["HADOOP_OPTS"] = (
            "-Djavax.net.ssl.trustStore=/var/lib/cloudera-scm-agent/agent-cert/cm-auto-global_truststore.jks -Djavax.net.ssl.trustStorePassword=Sbi@123"
        )

    @property
    def hdfs_filesystem(self) -> fs.HadoopFileSystem:
        """Get PyArrow HadoopFileSystem instance.

        Returns
        -------
        fs.HadoopFileSystem
            Configured HadoopFileSystem instance for HDFS operations.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> filesystem = client.hdfs_filesystem
        >>> file_info = filesystem.get_file_info("/user/data")
        """
        config = {
          "ssl.client.truststore.location": "/var/lib/cloudera-scm-agent/agent-cert/cm-auto-global_truststore.jks",
          "ssl.client.truststore.password": "Sbi@123",
          "ssl.client.truststore.type": "jks",
          "hadoop.rpc.socket.factory.class.default": "org.apache.hadoop.net.StandardSocketFactory"
        }
        _ = fs.HadoopFileSystem(host=self.host, extra_conf=config)
        return _

    def list_files(self, path: str) -> list[fs.FileInfo]:
        """List all files in the specified HDFS path recursively.

        Parameters
        ----------
        path : str
            HDFS path to list files from.

        Returns
        -------
        list[fs.FileInfo]
            List of FileInfo objects containing file metadata.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> files = client.list_files("/user/data")
        >>> for file in files:
        ...     print(f"{file.path}: {file.size} bytes")
        """
        selector = fs.FileSelector(path, recursive=True)
        files = self.hdfs_filesystem.get_file_info(selector)
        return [file for file in files]

    def upload(self, source_path: str, destination_path: str, overwrite: bool = True):
        """Upload a file from local filesystem to HDFS.

        Parameters
        ----------
        source_path : str
            Local file path to upload.
        destination_path : str
            HDFS destination path.
        overwrite : bool, optional
            Overwrite existing file if it exists. Default is True.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> client.upload("local_data.csv", "/user/data/remote_data.csv")
        >>>
        >>> # Upload without force overwrite
        >>> client.upload("model.pkl", "/user/models/model.pkl", overwrite=False)

        Notes
        -----
        Uses HDFS CLI command with -f flag to force overwrite if file exists.
        PyArrow filesystem is not used due to authentication issues.
        """
        # TODO - Ideally pyarrow.fs should be used but currently there is
        # a issue with auth & only hdfs cli is working
        if overwrite:
            subprocess.run(["hdfs", "dfs", "-put", "-f", source_path, destination_path], check=False)
        else:
            subprocess.run(["hdfs", "dfs", "-put", source_path, destination_path], check=False)

    def download(self, source_path: str, destination_path: str):
        """Download a file from HDFS to local filesystem.

        Parameters
        ----------
        source_path : str
            HDFS file path to download.
        destination_path : str
            Local destination path.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> client.download("/user/data/remote_data.csv", "local_data.csv")
        >>>
        >>> # Download to specific directory
        >>> client.download("/user/models/model.pkl", "./models/model.pkl")

        Notes
        -----
        Uses HDFS CLI command for downloading.
        PyArrow filesystem is not used due to authentication issues.
        """
        # TODO - Ideally pyarrow.fs should be used but currently there is
        # a issue with auth & only hdfs cli is working
        subprocess.run(["hdfs", "dfs", "-get", source_path, destination_path], check=False)

    def read_parquet_table(self, source_path: str) -> ds.FileSystemDataset:
        """Read parquet files from HDFS as a PyArrow dataset.

        Parameters
        ----------
        source_path : str
            HDFS path to parquet file or directory containing parquet files.

        Returns
        -------
        ds.FileSystemDataset
            PyArrow FileSystemDataset for the parquet data.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> dataset = client.read_parquet_table("/user/data/parquet_files")
        >>>
        >>> # Convert to pandas DataFrame
        >>> df = dataset.to_table().to_pandas()
        >>>
        >>> # Read with filters
        >>> table = dataset.to_table(filter=ds.field("year") == 2024)
        >>> df = table.to_pandas()
        """
        return ds.dataset(
            source=source_path, filesystem=self.hdfs_filesystem, format="parquet"
        )

    def read_csv_file(self, source_path: str) -> ds.FileSystemDataset:
        """Read CSV files from HDFS as a PyArrow dataset.

        Parameters
        ----------
        source_path : str
            HDFS path to CSV file or directory containing CSV files.

        Returns
        -------
        ds.FileSystemDataset
            PyArrow FileSystemDataset for the CSV data.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> dataset = client.read_csv_file("/user/data/csv_files")
        """
        return ds.dataset(
            source=source_path, filesystem=self.hdfs_filesystem, format="csv"
        )

    def load_model_sentence_transformer(self, model_path: str, **kwargs):
        """Load a SentenceTransformer model from HDFS.

        Downloads the model from HDFS to a temporary directory and loads it
        using the sentence-transformers library.

        Parameters
        ----------
        model_path : str
            HDFS path to the SentenceTransformer model directory.
        **kwargs : dict
            Additional keyword arguments to pass to SentenceTransformer constructor.

        Returns
        -------
        SentenceTransformer
            Loaded SentenceTransformer model instance.

        Raises
        ------
        ImportError
            If sentence-transformers library is not installed.
        ValueError
            If model requires trust_remote_code=True (automatically handled).

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> model = client.load_model_sentence_transformer(
        ...     "/dev/aiml/models/google/embeddinggemma4"
        ... )
        >>> embeddings = model.encode(["Hello world", "How are you?"])

        >>> # Load with onnx device backend
        >>> model = client.load_model_sentence_transformer(
        ...     "/dev/aiml/models/google/embeddinggemma4",
        ...     backend="onnx"
        ... )

        Notes
        -----
        The model is downloaded to a temporary directory which is automatically
        cleaned up after loading. If the model requires trust_remote_code=True,
        it will be automatically enabled with a warning.
        """
        try:
            from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer
        except ImportError:
            raise ImportError(
                "`sentence-transformers` library is not installed, please install it"
            )

        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
            local_model_path = f"{temp_dir}/model_dir"
            logger.debug("downloading the model from hdfs")
            self.download(model_path, local_model_path)
            logger.debug(
                f"successfully downloaded model temporarily to: {local_model_path}"
            )
            try:
                logger.info(f"loading model from {local_model_path}")
                model = SentenceTransformer(local_model_path, **kwargs)
            except ValueError as e:
                if "trust_remote_code=True" in str(e):
                    logger.warning(
                        "the model need to trust & execute custom code, enabling it"
                    )
                    model = SentenceTransformer(
                        local_model_path, trust_remote_code=True, **kwargs
                    )
                else:
                    raise

        return model

    def load_model_cross_encoder(self, model_path: str, **kwargs):
        """Load a CrossEncoder model from HDFS.

        Downloads the model from HDFS to a temporary directory and loads it
        using the sentence-transformers library.

        Parameters
        ----------
        model_path : str
            HDFS path to the CrossEncoder model directory.
        **kwargs : dict
            Additional keyword arguments to pass to CrossEncoder constructor.

        Returns
        -------
        CrossEncoder
            Loaded CrossEncoder model instance.

        Raises
        ------
        ImportError
            If sentence-transformers library is not installed.
        ValueError
            If model requires trust_remote_code=True (automatically handled).

        Examples
        --------
        >>> client = HDFSClient()
        >>> model = client.load_model_cross_encoder(
        ...     "/dev/aiml/models/jina/jina-reranker-multilingual-base-v2"
        ... )
        >>> scores = model.predict([
        ...     ("Query 1", "Document 1"),
        ...     ("Query 2", "Document 2")
        ... ])
        >>>
        >>> # Load with custom settings
        >>> model = client.load_model_cross_encoder(
        ...     "/dev/aiml/models/jina/jina-reranker-multilingual-base-v2",
        ...     max_length=512
        ... )

        Notes
        -----
        The model is downloaded to a temporary directory which is automatically
        cleaned up after loading. If the model requires trust_remote_code=True,
        it will be automatically enabled with a warning.
        """
        try:
            from sentence_transformers import CrossEncoder
        except ImportError:
            raise ImportError(
                "`sentence-transformers` library is not installed, please install it"
            )

        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
            local_model_path = f"{temp_dir}/model_dir"
            logger.debug("downloading the model from hdfs")
            self.download(model_path, local_model_path)
            logger.debug(
                f"successfully downloaded model temporarily to: {local_model_path}"
            )
            try:
                logger.info(f"loading model from {local_model_path}")
                model = CrossEncoder(local_model_path, **kwargs)
            except ValueError as e:
                if "trust_remote_code=True" in str(e):
                    logger.warning(
                        "the model need to trust & execute custom code, enabling it"
                    )
                    model = CrossEncoder(
                        local_model_path, trust_remote_code=True, **kwargs
                    )
                else:
                    raise

        return model



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\utils\config.py
####################################################################################################




"""
utils/config.py — application settings and shared constants.

  - (Pydantic BaseSettings — loads from .env)
  - (application-wide literal constants)

All secrets (API keys) must be provided via .env or environment variables.
TOML config files under genai/config/ provide default values;
environment variables always take precedence.
"""

from pydantic import Field
from pydantic_settings import BaseSettings, SettingsConfigDict

# SQL TAG CONSTANTS
SQL_BEGIN_TAG: str = "<begin_sql_response>"
SQL_END_TAG: str   = "<end_sql_response>"

COMPLIANCE_TABLE_NAME: str   = "COMP_TBL"
BRANCH_CODE_PLACEHOLDER: str = "{branch_code}"

RESPONSE_QUESTION_ID: str = "question_id"
RESPONSE_SQL_QUERY: str   = "sql_query"
RESPONSE_DF_OUTPUT: str   = "df_output"

LOGGER_ROOT_NAME: str = "compliance_bot"

DEFAULT_MAX_NEW_TOKENS: int       = 512   # maximum tokens the LLM will generate
DEFAULT_TEMPERATURE: float        = 0.1   # low temperature = more deterministic SQL
DEFAULT_STOP_SEQUENCES: list[str] = [SQL_END_TAG]  # stop generating at the end tag


# SETTINGS CLASSES

class DatabaseSettings(BaseSettings):
    """Iceberg / Hive data-connection configuration — read from environment or .env."""

    db_type:         str = Field(default="Iceberg",                    description="Database engine type.")
    iceberg_table:   str = Field(default="dev_feature_store.COMP_TBL", description="Fully-qualified Iceberg table name.")
    connection_name: str = Field(default="DLH-DEV-UAT - Hive on Tez",  description="CML Data connection name for PandasEngine.")


class ModelSettings(BaseSettings):
    """LLM connection and generation settings — read from environment or .env."""

    # extra="ignore" so unknown env vars don't cause validation errors
    model_config = SettingsConfigDict(extra="ignore")

    # Which LLM provider to use — always "dlh" for the on-prem deployment
    provider: str = Field(default="dlh", description="LLM provider (dlh).")

    # Model identifier sent in every chat-completions request
    model_name: str = Field(
        default="google/gemma-4-31b-it",
        description="Remote model name for the DLH OpenAI-compatible endpoint.",
    )

    # Generation caps — can be overridden per-request in ModelLoaderService.generate()
    model_max_new_tokens: int   = Field(default=512, description="Maximum tokens to generate.")
    model_temperature:    float = Field(default=0.1, description="Sampling temperature.")

    # DLH inference server connection details
    dlh_base_url: str = Field(
        default="http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1",
        description="DLH inference server base URL.",
    )
    dlh_api_key:    str  = Field(default="EMPTY", description="Bearer token for the DLH endpoint.")
    dlh_verify_ssl: bool = Field(default=False,   description="Verify TLS certificates for DLH.")


class AppSettings(BaseSettings):
    """Top-level application settings — merges .env, environment variables, and defaults."""

    model_config = SettingsConfigDict(
        env_file=".env",            # load from .env if it exists
        env_file_encoding="utf-8",
        env_nested_delimiter="__",  # DATABASE__DB_NAME sets database.db_name
        extra="ignore",             # silently ignore unknown env vars
        populate_by_name=True,
    )

    # Application display name shown in the Streamlit page title
    application_name: str  = Field(default="COMPLIANCE-BOT (UI + LLM Hybrid)")
    page_config:      str  = Field(default="SBI COMPLIANCE-BOT")

    # Logging level — can be overridden by LOG_LEVEL env var
    log_level: str = Field(default="INFO")
    log_dir:   str = Field(default="data/logs")

    # Server binding — unused in Streamlit mode but kept for reference
    host:  str  = Field(default="0.0.0.0")
    port:  int  = Field(default=8000)
    debug: bool = Field(default=False)

    # Nested settings — each reads its own env vars via the __ delimiter
    database: DatabaseSettings = Field(default_factory=DatabaseSettings)
    model:    ModelSettings    = Field(default_factory=ModelSettings)


# SINGLETON ACCESSOR

_settings: AppSettings = AppSettings()


def get_settings() -> AppSettings:
    """Return the cached AppSettings singleton.  Import-time cost is paid once."""
    return _settings



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\utils\helpers.py
####################################################################################################








"""
utils/helpers.py — stateless utility helpers.

  - (safe_json_loads, truncate, normalise_whitespace, flatten_list)
  - (ensure_directory, read/write text/bytes, safe_delete, list_files etc)
  - (safe_loads, safe_dumps, extract_json_from_text, etc)

no external dependencies beyond the
Python standard library.
"""

import json
import re
import shutil
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any


def safe_json_loads(text: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
    """Try to parse text as JSON; return None if parsing fails."""
    try:
        return json.loads(text)
    except (json.JSONDecodeError, TypeError):
        return None


def truncate(text: str, max_len: int = 300, suffix: str = "…") -> str:
    """Shorten text to max_len characters and append suffix if it was cut."""
    if len(text) <= max_len:
        return text
    return text[: max_len - len(suffix)] + suffix


def normalise_whitespace(text: str) -> str:
    """Collapse any run of whitespace (spaces, tabs, newlines) into a single space."""
    return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text).strip()


def flatten_list(nested: list[Any]) -> list[Any]:
    """
    Flatten exactly one level of nesting in a list.

    Example: [[1, 2], [3], 4] → [1, 2, 3, 4]
    """
    result: list[Any] = []
    for item in nested:
        if isinstance(item, list):
            result.extend(item)
        else:
            result.append(item)
    return result


def ensure_directory(path: str | Path) -> Path:
    """Create the directory (and any missing parents) if it doesn't exist yet."""
    resolved = Path(path).resolve()
    resolved.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    return resolved


def read_text(path: str | Path, encoding: str = "utf-8") -> str:
    """Read a text file and return its contents as a string."""
    return Path(path).read_text(encoding=encoding)


def write_text(path: str | Path, content: str, encoding: str = "utf-8") -> Path:
    """Write content to a file, creating parent directories if needed."""
    dest = Path(path).resolve()
    dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    dest.write_text(content, encoding=encoding)
    return dest


def read_bytes(path: str | Path) -> bytes:
    """Read a file and return its raw bytes."""
    return Path(path).read_bytes()


def write_bytes(path: str | Path, data: bytes) -> Path:
    """Write binary data to a file, creating parent directories if needed."""
    dest = Path(path).resolve()
    dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    dest.write_bytes(data)
    return dest


def safe_delete(path: str | Path) -> bool:
    """
    Delete a file or entire directory tree without raising if it doesn't exist.

    Returns True if something was deleted, False if the path was already gone.
    """
    target = Path(path)
    if not target.exists():
        return False
    if target.is_dir():
        shutil.rmtree(target)
    else:
        target.unlink()
    return True


def list_files(
    directory: str | Path,
    pattern: str = "*",
    recursive: bool = False,
) -> list[Path]:
    """
    Return a sorted list of files matching pattern inside directory.

    Set recursive=True to search all subdirectories as well.
    """
    root = Path(directory)
    globber = root.rglob if recursive else root.glob
    return sorted(p for p in globber(pattern) if p.is_file())


def file_size(path: str | Path) -> int:
    """Return the byte size of a file, or 0 if the file does not exist."""
    target = Path(path)
    return target.stat().st_size if target.exists() else 0


def resolve_project_path(*parts: str) -> Path:
    """
    Build an absolute path relative to the project root.

    The project root is one level above utils/ (workspace root).
    Example: resolve_project_path("data", "comp_db", "COMP_REPORTS.db")
    """
    project_root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
    return project_root.joinpath(*parts)


_JSON_OBJECT_PATTERN: re.Pattern[str] = re.compile(r"(\{.*?\}|\[.*?\])", re.DOTALL)


def safe_loads(text: str) -> Any:
    """Parse text as JSON; return None instead of raising on bad input."""
    try:
        return json.loads(text)
    except (json.JSONDecodeError, TypeError):
        return None


def safe_dumps(obj: Any, indent: int = 2, default: Any = str) -> str:
    """Serialise obj to JSON, converting un-serialisable values with str()."""
    return json.dumps(obj, indent=indent, default=default, ensure_ascii=False)


def extract_json_from_text(text: str) -> Any:
    """
    Scan free-form text for the first valid JSON object or array and return it.

    Useful when the LLM wraps its JSON answer in explanatory prose.
    Returns None if no valid JSON block is found.
    """
    for match in _JSON_OBJECT_PATTERN.finditer(text):
        parsed = safe_loads(match.group(0))
        if parsed is not None:
            return parsed
    return None


def extract_json_array(text: str) -> list[Any]:
    """Return the first JSON array found in text, or [] if none exists."""
    result = extract_json_from_text(text)
    if isinstance(result, list):
        return result
    return []


def extract_json_object(text: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Return the first JSON object found in text, or {} if none exists."""
    result = extract_json_from_text(text)
    if isinstance(result, dict):
        return result
    return {}


def pretty_print(obj: Any) -> str:
    """Return a nicely indented JSON string for obj."""
    return safe_dumps(obj, indent=2)


def is_valid_json(text: str) -> bool:
    """Return True if text is syntactically valid JSON, False otherwise."""
    return safe_loads(text) is not None



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\utils\logger.py
####################################################################################################




"""
utils/logger.py — centralised logging factory for the Compliance Chatbot.

All modules must obtain their logger exclusively through get_logger() to
guarantee consistent formatting and handler configuration.

Configuration is loaded from genai/config/logging.toml (Python 3.11+ tomllib).
Falls back to a minimal basicConfig when the TOML file is absent or unreadable.
"""

import logging
import logging.config
import logging.handlers
from pathlib import Path

_LOGGING_CONFIGURED: bool = False

_PROJECT_ROOT:     Path = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
_TOML_CONFIG_PATH: Path = _PROJECT_ROOT / "genai" / "config" / "logging.toml"


def _load_toml_logging_config(path: Path) -> dict[str, object] | None:
    """
    Parse a TOML file and return the [logging] section as a dict.

    Returns None on any failure (file missing, tomllib not available, parse error).
    The caller falls back to basicConfig when this returns None.
    """
    try:
        try:
            import tomllib  # built-in on Python 3.11+
        except ImportError:
            try:
                # tomli is the third-party back-port for Python < 3.11
                import tomli as tomllib  # type: ignore[no-redef]
            except ImportError:
                return None  # neither available — signal fallback

        # Open in binary mode as required by both tomllib and tomli
        with path.open("rb") as fh:
            data = tomllib.load(fh)
        # Return just the [logging] section — dictConfig expects this shape
        return data.get("logging")
    except Exception:
        # Any failure (missing file, parse error) → fall back to basicConfig
        return None


def _configure_logging(log_dir: str | None = None) -> None:
    """
    Bootstrap the root logger from genai/config/logging.toml.

    This function is guarded by _LOGGING_CONFIGURED so it runs at most once.
    Falls back to basicConfig if the TOML file is absent or unreadable.

    Args:
        log_dir: Optional override for the log output directory.
                 Used only on the very first call.
    """
    global _LOGGING_CONFIGURED  # pylint: disable=global-statement

    # Skip all setup if logging has already been configured in this process
    if _LOGGING_CONFIGURED:
        return

    # Resolve the log directory to an absolute path anchored at the project root.
    # This ensures data/logs/ is always created relative to dlh_ai/ regardless of
    # the working directory Streamlit was launched from.
    raw_dir          = Path(log_dir) if log_dir else Path("data/logs")
    resolved_log_dir = raw_dir if raw_dir.is_absolute() else _PROJECT_ROOT / raw_dir
    resolved_log_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)  # create dir if missing

    # Try to load the TOML-based logging configuration
    log_config = _load_toml_logging_config(_TOML_CONFIG_PATH)

    if log_config:
        # Patch the rotating_file handler's filename to the resolved directory
        # so the log file always lands in data/logs/ regardless of CWD.
        handlers = log_config.get("handlers", {})
        rotating = handlers.get("rotating_file")
        if rotating:
            rotating["filename"] = str(resolved_log_dir / "compliance_bot.log")

        try:
            # Apply the full dictConfig — sets up all handlers and loggers
            logging.config.dictConfig(log_config)
            _LOGGING_CONFIGURED = True
            return
        except Exception:
            # dictConfig failed (e.g. bad handler class) — fall through to basicConfig
            logging.getLogger(__name__).debug(
                "dictConfig failed — falling back to basicConfig."
            )

    # Fallback: minimal console-only logging when TOML is unavailable
    logging.basicConfig(
        level=logging.INFO,
        format="%(asctime)s | %(levelname)-8s | %(name)s | %(message)s",
    )
    logging.getLogger(__name__).warning(
        "Logging TOML config not found or unreadable at %s — using basicConfig fallback.",
        _TOML_CONFIG_PATH,
    )

    _LOGGING_CONFIGURED = True


def get_logger(name: str, log_dir: str | None = None) -> logging.Logger:
    """
    Return a named logger, bootstrapping the logging system on first call.

    Args:
        name:    Dot-separated logger hierarchy, e.g. "compliance_bot.core.model".
                 Use the module's logical path so log records can be filtered by prefix.
        log_dir: Optional override for the log directory; effective only on the
                 very first call to this function in the process.

    Returns:
        A fully configured logging.Logger instance.
    """
    # Ensure handlers and formatters are set up before any log record is emitted
    _configure_logging(log_dir=log_dir)
    return logging.getLogger(name)



####################################################################################################
FILE: dlh_ai\utils\registry.py
####################################################################################################


"""
utils/registry.py — data-access registries: databases, prompts, SQLite helpers.

  - databases.toml metadata registry (tables, columns, few-shots)
  - read-only SQLite connection + query helpers
  - YAML prompt file loader with in-memory cache

All three share the same concern: reading persistent data stores (TOML files
and SQLite databases) and caching the results in memory.
"""

import contextlib
import sqlite3
from collections.abc import Generator
from contextlib import contextmanager
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import yaml

from .logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.utils.registry")


def _load_toml_file(path: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Open and parse a TOML file; return {} on any failure."""
    try:
        try:
            import tomllib  # built-in Python 3.11+
        except ImportError:
            import tomli as tomllib  # type: ignore[no-redef]
        with path.open("rb") as fh:
            return tomllib.load(fh)
    except Exception as exc:
        LOGGER.warning("registry._load_toml_file — failed to load %s: %s", path, exc)
        return {}


_DATABASES_TOML = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "genai" / "config" / "databases.toml"

_db_registry: dict[str, Any] | None = None


def _build_db_registry() -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Parse databases.toml and return a flat {table_name: metadata} dict."""
    if not _DATABASES_TOML.exists():
        LOGGER.warning("registry: databases.toml not found at %s", _DATABASES_TOML)
        return {}

    raw = _load_toml_file(_DATABASES_TOML)
    registry: dict[str, Any] = {}

    for db_stem, db_data in raw.get("databases", {}).items():
        db_file = db_data.get("db_file", "")
        for table_name, table_data in db_data.get("tables", {}).items():
            few_shots_raw = table_data.get("few_shots", {})
            few_shots_str = (
                few_shots_raw.get("examples", "").strip()
                if isinstance(few_shots_raw, dict)
                else ""
            )
            registry[table_name] = {
                "db_stem":      db_stem,
                "db_file":      db_file,
                "description":  table_data.get("description", ""),
                "filter_query": table_data.get("filter_query", ""),
                "columns":      table_data.get("columns", {}),
                "few_shots":    few_shots_str,
            }
            LOGGER.debug("registry: registered table %s (db=%s)", table_name, db_stem)

    LOGGER.info("registry: loaded %d table(s) from databases.toml", len(registry))
    return registry


def _get_db_registry() -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Return the cached registry, building it on first call."""
    global _db_registry
    if _db_registry is None:
        _db_registry = _build_db_registry()
    return _db_registry


def reload() -> None:
    """Clear the database registry cache so the next access re-reads databases.toml."""
    global _db_registry
    _db_registry = None
    LOGGER.info("registry: db cache cleared — will reload on next access.")


def get_table_meta(table_name: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Return the full metadata dict for a registered table, or {} if unknown."""
    return _get_db_registry().get(table_name, {})


def get_few_shots(table_name: str) -> str:
    """Return the few-shot example block for a table, or '' if not registered."""
    return _get_db_registry().get(table_name, {}).get("few_shots", "")


def get_all_tables() -> list[str]:
    """Return a sorted list of every registered table name."""
    return sorted(_get_db_registry().keys())


def list_databases() -> list[str]:
    """Return a sorted list of every registered database stem name."""
    reg = _get_db_registry()
    return sorted({meta["db_stem"] for meta in reg.values()})


@contextmanager
def open_readonly(db_path: str | Path) -> Generator[sqlite3.Connection, None, None]:
    """
    Context manager: open a SQLite file in deferred read-only mode.

    Always rolls back and closes the connection on exit — even on error.
    Usage:
        with open_readonly("data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db") as conn:
            rows = conn.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL").fetchone()
    """
    path = Path(db_path)
    conn: sqlite3.Connection | None = None
    try:
        conn = sqlite3.connect(str(path), check_same_thread=False)
        conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        conn.execute("BEGIN DEFERRED")
        yield conn
    finally:
        if conn is not None:
            with contextlib.suppress(sqlite3.Error):
                conn.rollback()
            conn.close()


def fetch_all(
    db_path: str | Path,
    sql: str,
    params: tuple[Any, ...] = (),
) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    """Run sql and return every result row as a list of {column: value} dicts."""
    with open_readonly(db_path) as conn:
        cursor = conn.execute(sql, params)
        col_names: list[str] = [d[0] for d in (cursor.description or [])]
        return [dict(zip(col_names, row, strict=False)) for row in cursor.fetchall()]


def fetch_scalar(
    db_path: str | Path,
    sql: str,
    params: tuple[Any, ...] = (),
    default: Any = None,
) -> Any:
    """Run sql and return just the first column of the first row (e.g. COUNT(*))."""
    with open_readonly(db_path) as conn:
        row = conn.execute(sql, params).fetchone()
    return row[0] if row else default


def list_tables(db_path: str | Path) -> list[str]:
    """Return a sorted list of all table names in the database."""
    sql = "SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' ORDER BY name"
    with open_readonly(db_path) as conn:
        rows = conn.execute(sql).fetchall()
    return [row[0] for row in rows]


def table_exists(db_path: str | Path, table_name: str) -> bool:
    """Return True if table_name exists in the database (case-insensitive check)."""
    sql = "SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' AND UPPER(name)=UPPER(?)"
    return fetch_scalar(db_path, sql, (table_name,)) is not None


def get_column_names(db_path: str | Path, table_name: str) -> list[str]:
    """Return the ordered column names for a table via PRAGMA table_info."""
    with open_readonly(db_path) as conn:
        rows = conn.execute(f"PRAGMA table_info('{table_name}')").fetchall()
    return [row[1] for row in rows]


def row_count(db_path: str | Path, table_name: str) -> int:
    """Return how many rows are in the table; returns 0 on any error."""
    try:
        return fetch_scalar(db_path, f"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM '{table_name}'", default=0)
    except sqlite3.Error as err:
        LOGGER.warning("registry.row_count — error on %s.%s: %s", db_path, table_name, err)
        return 0


_PROMPTS_DIR: Path = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "genai" / "prompt"

_prompt_cache: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}


def _load_yaml_file(path: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Open and parse a YAML file; return {} on any failure."""
    try:
        with path.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
            return yaml.safe_load(fh) or {}
    except Exception as exc:
        LOGGER.warning("registry._load_yaml_file — failed to load %s: %s", path, exc)
        return {}


def load_prompt(filename: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """
    Load and cache a YAML prompt file from genai/prompt/.

    filename — e.g. "sql_writer.yaml" (or bare stem "sql_writer")
    Returns the full parsed dict, or {} on error.
    Raises FileNotFoundError if the file does not exist.
    """
    stem = filename.removesuffix(".yaml").removesuffix(".toml")

    if stem in _prompt_cache:
        return _prompt_cache[stem]

    path = _PROMPTS_DIR / (stem + ".yaml")
    if not path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Prompt file not found: {path}")

    data = _load_yaml_file(path)
    _prompt_cache[stem] = data
    LOGGER.debug("registry: loaded prompt file: %s", path)
    return data


def get_prompt(filename: str, *keys: str, default: str | None = None) -> str | None:
    """
    Retrieve a nested string value from a YAML prompt file.

    Example: get_prompt("intent_checker.yaml", "messages", "clarification")

    Walks the key path and returns the string at that location,
    or default if any key is missing.
    """
    data = load_prompt(filename)
    node: Any = data
    for key in keys:
        if not isinstance(node, dict):
            return default
        node = node.get(key)
        if node is None:
            return default
    return str(node) if node is not None else default
